Verification: XcodeGen and Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0.

## Session flow redesign revised (2026-11-18)

At the product owner's request, the proposal in *Session flow redesign (proposed)* was reviewed against the current source and revised. It is still proposed; nothing is implemented. See [Documentation/SessionFlowRedesign/README.md](SessionFlowRedesign/README.md).

- **Core and optional phases.** Phases 0–4 are the core milestone. When Phase 4 lands, the guided `SessionEngine` is a single-owner state machine: `SessionStage` cases carry their own data, there is one `Suspension`, one `GuidanceGate` per stage and one `record`, and `SessionProgress` no longer owns a stage. The redesign may stop there. Phases 5–9 (activity facts, guidance policy, finger, Balancing, daily plan) are independent follow-ups, each listing the phases it requires.
- **Countdown resume settled.** The guided countdown restarts after a resume and the finger countdown continues; ADR-010 records this when Phase 3 lands. It is no longer an open risk.
- **Bug 2 may land early.** Skip during a pause or camera wait may be fixed on its own before Phase 3, so that skip records the outcome and never lifts a suspension.
- **Composition wording aligned** with *Simplified architecture* and *Routers own navigation state*: builders, `…Dependency` protocols and routers replace the stale `AppDependencies` references. Phase 9 now uses the existing `DailyPlanNextViewModel` (built by `HomeBuilder`) instead of a new `LongRestViewModel`, routes through `DailyPlanRouterProtocol`, and adds `sessionEnded` to `DailyPlanSessionReporting`.
- Line references and file sizes in the proposal were refreshed against the current source.

Verification: documentation only; no code or runtime behaviour changed.

## Shared breathing rest (2026-11-18)

Phase 1 of the session flow redesign. The scripted short rest between sets existed twice, as `GuidedSetBreathing` (Indonesian arm raise, in `SessionEngine`) and `OppositionRest` (thumb opposition, in `FingerSessionViewModel`). The two had the same steps, keys, minimums (inhale 6 s, hold 2 s, exhale 3 s), 0.2 s credit cap and intensity curve. They are now one `BreathingRest` in `Therapy/Shared/SessionFlow/`. `tick(_:ready:)` keeps the finger path's `ready` gate; the guided path passes the default `true`. Timings and narration are unchanged.

`RestBreath` (the timed rest's 6 s / 3 s rhythm) stays as it is: it is a pure function of elapsed time. The proposed `Countdown` type moved to Phase 5, the first phase that uses it.

Verification: transcript harness (26 guided-session scenarios plus breathing timelines, including both rest openings and a suspend) identical to the Phase 0 baseline; XcodeGen and Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0. Pending on the M1 iPad: the scripted rest between arm-raise sets and between opposition sets.

## One guidance gate per cue (2026-11-18)

Phase 2 of the session flow redesign. A spoken cue's identity, narration and reading state and its watchdog are now one value, `GuidanceGate` (`Therapy/Shared/SessionFlow/`), instead of loose fields in three places:

- `SessionEngine`: `guidance`, `narrationComplete`, `readingComplete`, `speechTimeout` and `guidanceStartedAt` became `gate`.
- `FingerSessionViewModel`: `speechID` and `speechDeadline` became `speechGate`.
- `BalanceSessionViewModel`: `openingSpeechID` and `openingSpeechDeadline` became `openingGate`.

Stale and duplicate completions are ignored as before, and the watchdog values are unchanged: 45 s for scripted cues and feedback, the opening plan's watchdog or 20 s in the guided session, `max(15, characters × 0.25)` in finger sessions, 8 s for Balancing openings. The scripted follow-up cue (`pendingScriptMovement`) stays separate until Phase 6.

Verification: transcript harness, now built against a baseline worktree of the pre-refactor commit, identical across 29 scenarios. Three new scenarios replay every earlier completion and reading callback mid-session (generic and Indonesian-scripted). XcodeGen and Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0. The finger and Balancing changes are checked by build and review only, since their view models cannot run in the harness. Pending on the M1 iPad: Stop during narration then Go, with the next cue playing once, in a guided, a finger and a Balancing session; the finger watchdog after a narration failure; the Balancing opening advancing after each cue.

## Engine-owned suspension (2026-11-18)

Phase 3 of the session flow redesign. Adds **ADR-010 — Single session flow model**, accepted 2026-11-18. It refines §5 and supersedes nothing.

- **One owner.** The guided `SessionEngine` owns suspension through one `Suspension` value. It replaces three engine fields (`pausedSince`, `waitingSince`, `suspensionSince`) and `SessionProgress.pausedAt`.
- **Reasons and blockers.** Reasons freeze the session: `pause` (touch, "stop", Help, backgrounding, audio interruption) and `cameraWait`. Blockers only refuse Resume: `voiceUnavailable`, `storageFailure`. A newly reported blocker pauses.
- **The view model reports, the engine decides.** `TherapySessionViewModel` no longer decides whether Resume is allowed. Before forwarding a Resume it reports the current blockers (permissions, listening, audio capability, an unacknowledged save failure), and the engine ignores the Resume while any blocker remains. It also no longer pauses on its own: backgrounding, lost voice and a failed save are reported as blockers.
- **One frozen interval.** It opens with the first reason and closes when the engine resumes. Every deadline shifts by its length: the opening, preparation and feedback deadlines, and the rest deadline (`SessionProgress.shiftRest`).
- **Skip and Stop never lift a pause** (fixes bug 2 of the redesign). Before, a skip during a pause silently resumed the session. Now the set is recorded, the session stays paused, and its "skipped" feedback plays after Resume.
- **Countdown resume stays per path.** The guided countdown restarts after a resume; the finger countdown continues where it stopped.

Verification: transcript harness against the pre-refactor baseline, 30 scenarios. The only differences are skip-while-paused, which now stays paused until Resume and then speaks the skipped feedback, and the harness's own notes in the new blocker scenario (voice blocked while running pauses; Resume while blocked does nothing; Resume after clearing proceeds; identical state to the old view-model behaviour). XcodeGen and Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0. Pending on the M1 iPad: background and foreground in every stage; an incoming call; revoking the microphone mid-session; disabling on-device recognition; a failed save then Retry and Continue; Skip from the pause dialog (the dialog stays until Resume).

## Stages carry their data (2026-11-18)

Phase 4 of the session flow redesign, completing its core. Extends ADR-010: the guided session's stage is one value, `SessionStage` (Therapy/Session/Models), and each stage carries its own data.

- **Cases.** `.preparing`, `.opening` (step: calibration intro, scripted position or start, a card, or "try the movement"; its `advanceAt`), `.practice`, `.feedback` (`enteredAt`, `advanceAt`, whether the next set recalibrates), `.rest` (a timed deadline or scripted breathing, and whether the next set recalibrates), and `.summary`.
- **What it replaces.** The hand-synced `SessionProgress.Stage`, the engine's multipurpose `deadline`, `cardIndex`, `feedbackEnteredAt` and `calibrationInvalidatedBetweenSets`. Scripted breathing moved from the snapshot into the rest stage; the snapshot mirrors it.
- **Single transition point.** `enter(_:)` is the only place the stage changes, and `SessionSnapshot.stage` mirrors it. Leaving a stage drops its deadlines. The spoken cue's gate is not part of the stage: the next announcement replaces it.
- **One recording path.** Skip, completion and the caregiver's activity commands record through `record(_:)`, which enters feedback only when a result was actually added. Stop shares `recordResult(_:)` and then finalizes. `SessionProgress.record` no longer takes the (always current) execution identity and reports whether it recorded.
- **One replay path.** Resume and camera recovery use `resumeAfterSuspension()` and `replayStageCue(timed:)`. The two keep their existing differences: which stages replay, and whether the cue is timed.
- **Left for Phase 5:** `awaitingCountdownGuidance` and `calibrationCompletionPending` (the countdown and calibration-cue handshake), and the `"calibration.complete"` string check. `guidancePlan` and `positioningPending` remain as guidance settings.

No behaviour changed. The four bugs this phase addressed were unreachable or latent: the stale deadline after a skip was always cleared by the feedback's timed cue.

Verification: transcript harness against the pre-refactor baseline, 33 scenarios. New scenarios cover a skip while a card or demonstration deadline is pending, and a stop in the same turn as the repetition that completes the set (one outcome, one summary). The only differences remain Phase 3's skip-while-paused fix and harness notes. XcodeGen and Debug and Release Simulator builds with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0. Pending on the M1 iPad: a full two-set arm raise (generic and Indonesian script) with Pause in every stage; elbow bend and directional reach openings; fold towel; a camera change during rest followed by recalibration; Debug "Finish exercise" mid-plan.

## Activity facts and one countdown (2026-11-18)

Phase 5 of the session flow redesign, at the product owner's request, accepting countdown timing differences. Extends ADR-010.

- **Activities report facts.** Each guided activity reports what an event changed as `ActivityFact`s: countdown requested, movement phase change, repetition credited, target reached, towel fold or grab, cue change. `SessionEngine` reacts to those facts. It no longer compares the activity's old and new presentation, and no longer checks `"calibration.complete"`; the views read `snapshot.countdown` and `snapshot.speaksCalibrationComplete` instead. The arm-raise script's cue-key comparison remains until Phase 6.
- **The engine owns the countdown.** Before, each activity ran its own: arm raise and elbow bend from ticks and frames, directional reach from a deadline it set before the cue, fold towel from ticks, timed practice from frames only. Now the practice stage holds one `Countdown`:
  - its cue is spoken ("calibration complete" first when the set still owes it), and the countdown starts when the cue ends;
  - each second is announced once, on the exact second;
  - it ends on the first event at or after its end, and the engine sends `.countdownFinished` to the activity;
  - a pause or camera wait stops it, and it restarts from its full length after its cue is spoken again.
  Durations are unchanged: 3 s, or 5 s before a later scripted arm-raise set.

Timing and narration differences, confirmed in the transcript harness:
- Seconds are announced, and the countdown ends, exactly on the second, from any event. Before, this happened on the next tick or frame, up to 50 ms later. Later events in a session shift by the same few milliseconds.
- Directional reach shows 3 while its cue is spoken. Before, a deadline it set before the cue was already counting down.
- Timed practice can end its countdown on a tick, not only on a frame.
- When a countdown ends, practice starts the same way every time. The screen shows the practice cue at once instead of "session.countdown" for one more frame. The arm-raise script always speaks its first movement line. The generic "move to target" line is no longer spoken: before, it played only when a frame rather than a tick happened to end the countdown.
- The Indonesian script no longer speaks hold seconds over the countdown digits. Before, the countdown's progress ring was misread as a hold.
- A skip during calibration no longer makes the next set open with "calibration complete".
- During a pause in a countdown, the digit shows the full length, because the countdown restarts after Resume.
- The movement countdown's progress ring value is gone; the full-screen countdown shows only the digit.

Verification: transcript harness against the core-complete commit (016fd83), 37 scenarios. New scenarios cover a pause in each countdown, reach rehearsal into its countdown, fold towel into practice, and a timed-practice later set. The facts commit was identical to the baseline. The countdown commit differs only in the classes listed above, checked scenario by scenario with timestamps and narration identities set aside. XcodeGen and Debug and Release Simulator builds with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0. Pending on the M1 iPad: the countdown cue and digits for arm raise (generic, and scripted set 2 at 5 s), elbow bend, directional reach, fold towel and timed practice; Pause and camera loss during each countdown; the script's first movement line after the countdown, with practice continuing; a two-minute arm-raise practice with unchanged preview cadence and stale-frame logs, since facts must add no per-frame work.

## Finger sessions always listen (2026-11-18)

"stop" and "go" did nothing in finger and forearm sessions. `FingerSessionViewModel` enabled recognition only when the saved `FingerRoutine.voiceControl` was true. That value defaults to `false`, and its only setter, the `plan.voice` toggle in `FingerRoutineSettingsView`, was removed with the unreachable routine screens in *0b196ab* (2026-11-15). Any installation without a saved `true` therefore never started `GuidanceAudioService` recognition. The plan's readiness check still passed, because it validates `SessionAudioService`, not the finger service. This contradicted section 1 (command recognition is mandatory, with no touch-only bypass).

Finger sessions now always enable voice at start and after a camera retry. Backgrounding and audio interruptions always require voice to be acknowledged again. `voiceControl` stays in `FingerRoutine`, the routine file and finger session records, so stored data decodes unchanged, but it no longer gates anything.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0. Diagnosed from a device log that showed the readiness check's listening but no finger-session transcripts. Pending on the M1 iPad: "stop" pauses and "go" resumes in thumb opposition and a forearm session, during narration and in silence; listening returns after background and foreground and after an audio interruption.

## Voice control in Settings (2026-11-18)

Settings said nothing about voice control after its routine toggle was removed, though every session now listens (*Finger sessions always listen*). A Voice control section now follows Language in `TMPracticePreferencesView`:
- **The toggle mirrors permission.** It is on when microphone and speech recognition are both authorized, and switching it off does nothing.
- **A fallback appears when access is missing.** Switching it on, or tapping the button below it, runs that fallback:
  - never asked: Enable permissions requests the microphone, then speech recognition, through `PermissionService`;
  - denied: Open Settings, with the matching `home.microphoneDenied` or `home.speechDenied` message;
  - restricted: only the `home.restricted` message.
- **When granted,** the footer explains that it is always on, with "stop" and "go".
- **It stays current.** `PracticePreferencesViewModel` reads the observable permission statuses and refreshes them on appear and when the app becomes active, so returning from the system Settings updates the row.

Sessions are unchanged; they still validate permissions and listening at start. The camera is left to that check.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0. Pending: in the Simulator and on the M1 iPad, after resetting privacy, the toggle starts off with Enable permissions and turns on after granting both prompts; switching it off snaps back; a denied permission shows Open Settings and the row updates after returning; both languages and large text.

## Shared log manager (2026-11-18)

Logging was inconsistent. About 30 `NSLog` calls in Tracking, Audio, Storage and Session sat beside two private OSLog `Logger`s in `SessionAudioService` and `GuidanceAudioService`. None of them said where a line came from. `Shared/Logging/LogManager.swift` is now the app's one logging entry point:
- **One logger per area.** `LogCategory` has the cases `app`, `audio`, `voice`, `tracking`, `session` and `storage`. Each becomes an OSLog category under the bundle-identifier subsystem, so Console and `log stream` can filter by area.
- **Formatted lines.** Each line reads `2026-11-18T14:03:22.123+07:00 🔴 ERROR [storage] [bg] LocalSessionRepository.swift:78 summaries() › message`: a local ISO 8601 timestamp with milliseconds, a severity glyph and fixed-width level, the category, main or background thread, and the caller's file, line and function. `LogLevel` maps `debug`, `info`, `notice`, `warning`, `error` and `fault` onto `OSLogType`. `error(_:error:category:)` appends the error's domain, code and description.
- **Cost.** Messages are autoclosures. `debug` lines are dropped before formatting in Release, so per-frame call sites cost nothing there. The existing throttled stale-frame logs keep their throttles and log at `warning`. Messages stay `.public`, as before: they hold diagnostics, never patient data.
- **Reached directly.** `LogManager.shared` is the documented exception to injecting dependencies and to having no global dependencies. It holds only immutable values (a fixed table of `Logger`s and a value-type date style) and must work from Foundation-only engines and any thread. Injecting it through every initializer would add noise and provide no substitution boundary.

Every previous `NSLog` and private `Logger` call now goes through it. Their wording is unchanged apart from the appended error details.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0, plus a grep showing that no `NSLog(` or `Logger(` remains outside `LogManager.swift`. Pending: the formatted lines and category filtering in the Xcode console and on the M1 iPad, and an unchanged two-minute arm-raise preview cadence with stale-frame logs.

## Sit to stand without the ankle (2026-11-18)

At the product owner's request, sit to stand no longer needs the ankle. Patients sit sideways on a chair in front of the iPad, which frames them only down to the knees, so the ankle was never tracked and the exercise reported lost tracking throughout. `SitToStandActivity` now follows shoulder, hip and knee on the side facing the camera (confidence 0.3, leg selection unchanged):
- **Thigh elevation replaces the knee angle.** The knee-to-hip line above horizontal is near 0° seated and close to 90° standing. It ignores trunk lean, and with feet flat it follows knee extension (knee ≈ 90° + elevation). The seated check accepts −30° to 40° (previously knee 45°–145°); the trial still needs 25° of gain.
- **Hip rise replaces hip height above the ankle.** The hip's height in the frame per trunk length (shoulder–hip), compared with the seated pose. The trial still needs 0.15 of gain. This assumes a still camera during the session; Recalibrate covers a moved chair or camera.
- **Unchanged.** Rise still averages the two signals between the calibrated seated and standing poses, and lean, phases, dwell, the lean-first and slow-down cues and counting are unchanged. Hold tolerances scale with thigh plus trunk length instead of leg length; the leg guard is now a thigh of at least 0.06.
- **Recorded angle.** Key repetitions and the report record the shoulder–hip–knee angle (`hipAngle`) instead of the knee angle, under "Hip straightening" / "Pinggul lurus" (`history.report.hipExtension`, replacing `history.report.kneeExtension`). Sessions saved earlier hold knee angles in a similar 90°–180° range and now show under the new label.
- **Wording.** Strings that asked for the ankle or "head to feet" now ask for shoulder, hip and knee, or "head to knees". The full-body demonstration is unchanged.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0, and a temporary harness outside the repository feeding synthetic side-view frames without ankles. Pending: re-rendering the Marizka narration for `sts.trackingLost`, `sitToStand.tracking` and `session.assistance.sitToStand` (Indonesian and `--language en`, then `--check`), whose bundled clips still mention the ankle or feet; and, on the M1 iPad, a sideways patient framed to the knees facing either way through seated capture, trial, counting and the lean-first cue, in both languages.

## Smooth whole-hand circle (2026-11-18)

The whole-hand circle in full fist, hook fist and forearm rotation did not look smooth. It was the one finger indicator still drawn as a plain `TMTherapyGuideBubble(outlineOnly: true)` stroke rather than with the `mergingGuide` shader, and its geometry jumped: it was sized and centred from the bounding box of every visible joint, which shrank and shifted with each closing fist and jumped as joints crossed the 0.3 confidence threshold, and its diameter was rounded to 4 pt steps.
- **Shader circle.** `TMFingerLandmarkOverlay` now draws it with `TMTherapyMergingGuide(center:diameter:...)`. `TMTherapyMergingGuide` gained `filled` (default true; false keeps the active outline without the soft fill, so the hand stays fully visible), and its single-circle initializer now takes the completion identity, its callback and `popsOnCompletion`. The arc, pop and completion acknowledgment are unchanged. The ring is the merging guide's width (at most 14 pt instead of 20 pt), matching the other indicators.
- **Steady geometry.** The circle is centred on the middle knuckle and is 2.3 palm lengths (wrist to middle knuckle) across, unstepped, at least 100 pt, at most 75% of the shorter preview side and kept on screen as before. Both anchors are already filtered by `HandOverlaySmoother` and barely change while the fingers bend. The bounding box remains only as a fallback when the wrist or middle knuckle is not visible.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0. Pending on the M1 iPad: in full fist, hook fist and forearm rotation the circle stays still through opening, closing and turning and encloses the open hand with a spread thumb; it stays inside the preview near the edges; the arc, pop and acknowledgment at hold completion; Reduce Motion; the overlay keeps 30 fps.

## No instruction caption on the camera (2026-11-18)

The Finger, Balancing and light guided sessions showed a one-line instruction caption, white text on black, along the bottom edge of the camera video during practice. It is removed.
- **Views.** The three sessions no longer pass a `cue` to `TMTherapyExerciseLiveLayout`; they keep `cuePlacement: .video(...)`, which still sets the attached layout's counters, controls and demo-title offset. The cue builders that fed the caption are removed (`FingerSessionView.cue`/`liveCue`/`isTracking`, `BalanceSessionView.practiceCue`/`cueKey`, the light session's `lightCue`), with `FingerSessionViewModel.scriptedCueKey`, `liveFeedbackKey` and the write-only `cameraMessage`.
- **Design system.** `TMTherapyExerciseLiveLayout` draws no caption for the video placement, and `TMTherapyInstructionBubble` lost its `singleLine` and `attached` options. `TMTherapyVideoFooter` stays for the light session's calibration card.
- **Unchanged.** Narration, the guidance and demonstration cards, the calibration card, camera-wait and error notices, and the dark layout's footer.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build. Pending on the M1 iPad: no caption over the camera in a finger exercise, Balancing and an arm-raise light session, with counters, controls, the demo title and the calibration card unchanged.

## Bilateral exercises alternate sides (2026-11-18)

Balancing exercises set to both arms alternate after every repetition, but the guided exercises (arm raise, elbow bend, directional reach, fold towel) set to both sides ran every set on the first side, then every set on the other as a second activity. They now alternate as Balancing does.
- **Routine.** `RoutineExercise.activities` turns `.both` into one `SessionActivity` with `alternatesSides`, starting on `firstSide`. Its goal counts both sides together, as Balancing's does: a goal of 5 is L, R, L, R, L, where the plan previously asked 5 per side. One-side configurations are unchanged.
- **Activity.** `AlternatingSidesActivity` (Therapy/Shared/ActivityLifecycle) keeps one single-side activity per side and routes events to the active one, so calibration, holds and counting stay in the existing activities. While a set starts, a side that reaches its countdown hands over to the next uncalibrated side, and once both are ready the one countdown starts on the first side. A credited repetition is presented with its own side and the other side takes over on the next frame, so counts and key repetitions carry the side that moved. A side taking over meets stale evidence and holds its start first. `.nextSet` and `.recalibrate` restart on the first side. Directional reach merges both sides' outcomes, skipped steps and practice seconds into the result.
- **Engine.** `ActivityFact.sideChanged` updates `SessionSnapshot.activeSide`. A handover outside the countdown speaks `session.switchArm.left/right` (new strings, "Now your left arm." / "Sekarang lengan kiri.") or, for the towel, the recorded `recovery.left/right`; arm exercises then speak the new side's instruction. The end of the countdown names the starting side. The recorder receives the active side.
- **Views.** The side label, the demonstration mirroring, the towel's required hand and the reach demonstration pose follow `activeSide`. The set summary row shows `history.bothSides`.
- **Unchanged.** The Indonesian arm-raise briefs still run one arm per session, left then right; `ArmRaiseGuidance.applies` excludes alternating activities.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0. A temporary harness outside the repository drove `AlternatingSidesActivity` with stub sides. It confirmed that both sides calibrate before a single countdown, that repetitions alternate L, R, L, R, L with each credit on the side that moved, that the set ends at the shared goal, and that next set and recalibration restart on the first side. Pending: Marizka recordings of `session.switchArm.left/right` in both languages through `Tools/generate_narration.py` (the system voice speaks them until then). On the M1 iPad: alternating arm raise, elbow bend, reach and towel in both orientations and languages; the second side's calibration; the towel re-locking the other hand at each handover; Stop/Go during a side cue; the report's per-repetition sides; the Indonesian scripted arm raise still running one arm per session.

## Calibration intro for each side (2026-11-18)

In an alternating exercise the second side was calibrated, but it was introduced only by the spoken `session.switchArm.<side>` and its instruction, while the first side had the full calibration intro. Both sides now get the same intro.
- **Engine.** A handover to a side that still has to capture its positions (movement `.startCapture`, reach `.setup`) runs `beginSideCalibration()`: `calibration.intro`, then that side's cards, then capture, as readiness does for the first side. "Calibration complete" is still said once, before the single countdown after the last side. A handover during practice keeps the short side cue.
- **Guidance.** `SessionGuidance.opening` takes the side being calibrated (`snapshot.activeSide`) for its side-keyed cards (`session.opening.<kind>.<side>.ready`, `parity.example.<side>`, `towel.opening.<side>`). An alternating reach puts `session.switchArm.<side>` before `parity.reachOpening`, which names no arm.
- **Unchanged.** Recalibration (by command, a camera change, or before the next set) still recalibrates both sides from the first; the next set keeps both calibrations; the towel has no calibration.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0. A temporary harness outside the repository drove `AlternatingSidesActivity` over the real `ElbowBendActivity` with synthetic frames for both arms. It confirmed that no countdown is requested after the left arm alone; that the handover reaches the right arm at its start capture; that one countdown follows both captures, starting on the left; and that recalibration asks both arms again. It also confirmed the per-side and reach cards from `SessionGuidance.opening`. Pending on the M1 iPad: the second side's intro, cards and mirrored demonstration for elbow bend, arm raise and reach, then one countdown and alternating practice.

## Fold towel opening over the camera (2026-11-19)

Before practice, arm raise, elbow bend and directional reach kept the camera live and showed their opening and demonstration cards as the caption strip over the video, but fold towel covered the camera with full-screen light pages, and its opening card (`towel.opening.<side>`) had no recording, so the system voice read it.
- **Views.** `TherapyLightSession.usesCameraOpening` (was `usesCalibrationOpening`) includes fold towel, so its cards use the calibration card over the camera with counters hidden. The heading stays the exercise and side (`showsCalibrationHeading` excludes the towel), since the towel has no calibration and still skips `calibration.intro`. The engine is unchanged.
- **Strings.** The one-line strip could not fit `towel.demo`, so its screen text is shorter ("Jepit di lingkaran yang bersinar, geser, lalu buka tangan." / "Pinch on the glowing circle, slide, then open your hand."). New `speak.towel.demo` keeps the previous full wording for narration, so the existing recordings stay valid.
- **Narration.** `towel.opening.left/right` joined `BODY_KEYS` and were recorded with Marizka in both languages. Only these four clips were rendered and levelled: a full run re-rendered about 130 uncached Indonesian lines on this machine and was reverted.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0; the four clips and both strings are in the app bundle. Both English takes pass the calm gate (167 Hz and 177 Hz). `--check --language en` still fails on the earlier missing `session.switchArm.left/right` and the not-calm `shoulderFlexion.title`. Pending: the cards' look was not viewed, including whether `towel.opening` fits the strip at its 70% minimum scale. On the M1 iPad: the towel cards over the camera in both languages, the new clips' tone and pronunciation, cards advancing after narration, the second side's opening in an alternating towel, and Stop/Go during the cards.

## Responsive narration hand-off (2026-11-19)

Sessions felt unresponsive: the exercise indicator appeared only after narration finished plus fixed waits (3 s after each card, 8 s after a demonstration), and the countdown covered the camera, so the target was hidden until practice began. Following each state change with narration instead would cut cues mid-word or overlap them. Visuals now lead and narration hands over without a hard cut.

- **Shorter pauses.** `SessionGuidance.defaultQuietDelay` 3 → 1.5 s after a card; `SessionEngine.demonstrationHold` 8 → 3 s after a demonstration (the clip already plays under the narration). The capture plan keeps 1 s. Reading and VoiceOver acknowledgment still gate every card. Finger and Balancing had no fixed wait after a cue and are unchanged.
- **Quiet target before practice.** Until practice starts, during the cue before the countdown and under it, the target is drawn quietly (`TMSessionBubbleOverlay(preview:)`, the towel zone and the finger targets at 60%), then eases to live. The countdown stays the full-screen `TMPracticeCountdown` at the user's request; a compact badge over the camera was tried and removed.
- **Hand-off.** A new `SessionEffect.handOffSpeech` replaces `.cancelSpeech` where a cue is replaced because the session moved on: timed cards, live towel cues, scripted lines and the end of a narrated rest. `SessionAudioService.handOffSpeech()` lets a recorded line with at most 1 s left finish (the next cue queues behind it), and otherwise fades it over 0.25 s and starts the next cue 0.3 s later, with the music kept ducked. The system voice stops after its current word. Queued prompts and counts of the replaced cue are dropped; the `.guidance` prefixed cue hands off too and waits in `pendingGuidance`. `PreparedAudioClip.remaining(at:)` provides the time left. `GuidanceAudioService.speak` (finger sessions) hands off the same way: the current line finishes and the rest of the cue is dropped, or it fades. Balancing hands off at rest-breath steps, the next set and "calibration complete".
- **Unchanged.** Pause, Stop, voice "stop", interruptions, recalibration, camera loss, the watchdog and the end of a session still cut at once through `cancelSpeech`. Countdown numbers always cut. Gate identities already ignore the replaced cue's late completion.
- **Conflict noted.** §5 still requires narration to complete before a card advances; this entry keeps that rule. The plan's Phase D (short cues after the first set, a stuck re-prompt, and movement ending a card early) is not implemented. Short cues need approved wording and new Marizka recordings. Early card exit cannot work as planned, because opening cards run before calibration, when there is no target to detect movement against.

Verification: XcodeGen, then Debug and Release Simulator builds with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0. No transcript harness was run: the engine change only swaps effects and shortens two deadlines. Pending on the M1 iPad: hand-off sound (no click, no overlap, a natural pause) in a guided, a finger and a Balancing session in both languages; voice "stop" during narration still cuts at once and Go replays once; the full-screen countdown unchanged in arm raise, a finger exercise and Balancing, and the quiet target easing to live as it ends; the shorter card and demonstration pauses reading comfortably; the finger countdown fallback when a recorded number is missing.

## Body exercises do not wait for narration (2026-11-19)

Balancing never holds evidence back for narration once calibration starts: only its opening cues and countdown cue do. Two guided body exercises still did, so the patient waited for the voice before anything counted. Both now follow Balancing.
- **Indonesian arm-raise script.** `MovementActivity.waitingForGuidance` dropped every frame after each step (start or target captured, phase or count change, countdown end) until the next scripted line finished. It is removed: frames are consumed unless the activity is suspended, and `.guidanceCompleted` no longer affects movement. The step-change reset of `last`/`lastValid` stays, so each 3 s hold still starts from fresh samples. Lines follow the movement through the engine's existing instruction check. Because scripted cues now hand off (*Responsive narration hand-off*), a patient moving ahead of the voice hears the current line fade into the next one, not a queue. This supersedes the "narration-gated three-second holds" of the approved script (2026-11-14); timing, wording and the script's hold-second narration are unchanged.
- **Directional reach.** `awaitingGuidance` no longer blocks the start hold, and is no longer re-armed on resume or recalibration. Each direction's 8 s assessment window starts when its cue ends **or** when the wrist leaves the start bubble (`sharedBubbleRadius`), whichever comes first. A patient who moves during the cue is measured at once, and one who waits for it loses no window time.
- **Unchanged.** The opening cards, "try the movement", the Indonesian "calibration.start" line, "calibration complete" and the countdown cue still gate evidence, as Balancing's opening cues do. Generic arm raise and elbow bend and fold towel had no other gate. Thumb opposition (hand tracking) still waits for its scripted lines. Phase 6's planned `gatesScoringOnNarration` is dropped from `Phase6-GuidancePolicy.md`.

Verification: XcodeGen, then Debug and Release Simulator builds with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0. A temporary harness outside the repository (`swiftc` over the Foundation-only activity files) sent synthetic frames and never `.guidanceCompleted`. Scripted right arm raise: start and target captured, back to start, countdown, two repetitions to the goal; the target hold starts fresh after the start capture. Reach: the start hold completes; the window stays closed while the wrist rests during the cue, opens on leaving the start, and opens on cue completion without movement. The same harness against the previous commit fails 9 of 12 checks, as expected. Pending on the M1 iPad: Indonesian arm raise on both arms, moving ahead of the narration, with lines fading into the next without stacking and the hold seconds still matching the bubble; reach while its direction cue plays; Stop/Go during a scripted line; generic arm raise and elbow bend unchanged.

## Per-exercise sides and counts (2026-11-19)

At the product owner's request, each plan exercise is configured on its own, in Release builds too. Before, Settings had one plan-wide "Training side" (default, left or right), and per-exercise sets and repetitions existed only in the Debug plan editor.
- **Settings.** The finger-routine section is unchanged. A new "Each exercise" section lists every plan exercise as a `DisclosureGroup`: the label shows its side and counts, and inside are a side picker (Right, Left, Both sides; none for sit to stand), sets (1–5) and repetitions (1–20). Changes apply from the next session.
- **Storage.** UserDefaults per item id: `practice.side.<id>` (`RoutineSides`), `practice.sets.<id>`, `practice.repetitions.<id>`. `DailyPlanViewModel` keeps them in observed `sideChoices` and `countChoices`. Without a choice, an exercise keeps its program's own values: the finger routine's hand and counts, the arm or task configuration, the Balancing exercise's saved arm. A plan-wide side saved by an earlier build (`practice.dailyPlanSide`) carries over to every exercise without its own side.
- **Both sides.** Arm raise (English), elbow bend, reach, towel and Balancing alternate sides within one session, as before. A finger or forearm exercise on both hands runs as two sessions, left then right, with the long rest between. This generalizes the Indonesian arm-raise briefs' two sessions (`pendingArmRaiseSide` became `pendingSecondSide`, with `passes(for:)` and `passSide(for:)`). The item is marked done only after the last hand. The second pass reopens the item's intro on the other hand, so the thumb-opposition script and its long rest follow the hand.
- **Counts.** Settings' counts replace the program's own through the existing `customCounts` path. Arm movements and the towel set the goal per side, reach sets rounds, and finger and Balancing exercises set their sets and repetitions. The Indonesian scripts speak a recorded line only when its numbers are true, as with the former debug counts.
- **Debug editor.** `DebugPlanLayout` keeps only which exercises show and their order. Its custom counts and `debug.planEditor.customCounts` are removed, and counts saved by older Debug builds are ignored.
- **Strings.** New: `settings.planExercisesTitle`, `settings.planExercisesHelp`, `settings.side`, `settings.side.both`. Removed: `settings.trainingSide`, `settings.trainingSideDefault`. Reworded: `debug.planEditor.help`.

Verification: XcodeGen, then Debug and Release Simulator builds with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0. The Settings sheet was not viewed. Pending on the M1 iPad: the Settings list in both languages and with large text (labels, summaries, the rows expanding); choices persisting and showing on Beranda's cards; arm raise set to left only, right only and both (English alternates; Indonesian runs two briefs); a Balancing exercise on each side; thumb opposition on both hands (left session, long rest, right intro and session, item done only after the right hand); custom counts in a guided, a finger and a Balancing exercise; an upgrade from a saved plan-wide side.

## One Exercises section in Settings (2026-11-19)

At the product owner's request, plan ordering is available in Release, and Settings has one place for exercise preferences instead of three: the finger-routine section (sets, repetitions and rest of the whole finger routine), *Per-exercise sides and counts*' "Each exercise" section, and the Debug plan editor (order and visibility).
- **Layout in Release.** `DebugPlanLayout` became `DailyPlanLayout` (`Home/Models/DailyPlanLayout.swift`), compiled in every build and stored under `practice.planLayout`. A layout saved by the Debug editor (`debug.dailyPlanLayout`) is read once as a fallback, and its old count fields are ignored. `DailyPlanViewModel.layout`, `applyLayout(_:)` (still ignored while a plan runs) and `resetLayout()` replace the debug-only API. `DailyPlanLayout.moveEntries(fromOffsets:toOffset:)` reorders without SwiftUI.
- **Per-exercise rest.** `DailyPlanCounts` became `DailyPlanOverrides` with `restSeconds` (5–120 s, step 5; `practice.rest.<id>`). Without a choice it falls back to the finger routine's, the arm or task configuration's, or the Balancing exercise's saved rest. It applies to the guided `Routine(restSeconds:)`, `BalancePracticeSettings.restSeconds`, and finger sessions through `FingerSessionService.prepare(restSeconds:)` when no checkpoint is resumed. The Indonesian arm-raise script's breathing rest is unchanged.
- **Settings.** One "Exercises" / "Latihan" section, in the plan's order. Each exercise is a `DisclosureGroup` whose label shows side · sets · repetitions, or "Hidden". Inside are the side (except sit to stand), sets, repetitions and rest. "Arrange" / "Atur urutan" in the section header switches the list to drag handles with a show/hide switch per exercise and "Restore default order"; Done returns. Every change saves at once. The finger-routine section is gone; the saved finger routine still supplies the defaults.
- **Removed.** `TMDebugPlanEditorView`, `DebugPlanEditorViewModel`, `HomeBuilder.buildDebugPlanEditor` and `PracticePreferencesContentBuildable`. The Settings view is no longer generic over a builder, since nothing else used one. Also removed: `PracticePreferencesViewModel`'s `FingerHomeViewModel` dependency and its save-on-Done, and the strings `debug.planEditor.*`, `settings.exerciseTitle` and `settings.exerciseHelp`.
- **Debug tool.** `-TuntunScreen settings` opens the Settings sheet on launch.
- **Note.** The approved scripts assume thumb opposition opens the day ("Hari ini kita akan mulai…") and arm raise follows ("Selanjutnya…"). Reordering can put those intros out of their written order; that is the caregiver's choice.

Verification: XcodeGen, then Debug and Release Simulator builds with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0. No `DebugPlanLayout`, `debugLayout` or `planEditor` references remain. The string catalog differs from HEAD only in the four added, six removed and two reworded keys. A temporary harness over `DailyPlan.swift` and `DailyPlanLayout.swift` passed 11 checks: standard default, dragging down and up, save round trip, hidden items left out, unknown ids dropped, new items appended and shown, and the legacy debug layout carried over with its counts ignored. On the iPad Pro 13-inch Simulator, `-TuntunScreen settings` showed the Exercises section in plan order with each summary, and the Arrange button, now tinted, in the header. Arrange mode and an expanded row were not viewed, since the Simulator could not be tapped. Pending on the M1 iPad: Arrange (drag, hide, restore) and Beranda following it; expanded rows with large text; rest taking effect in a finger, a guided and a Balancing exercise; a hidden item left out of "today done"; layout changes ignored during a running plan.

## Camera rotation without deprecated orientation (2026-11-19)

The build's only code warnings were uses of `AVCaptureConnection.videoOrientation` / `isVideoOrientationSupported`, deprecated in iOS 17. They appeared in the hand and body previews and in the hand and body cameras' data outputs. They are replaced with rotation angles from `AVCaptureDevice.RotationCoordinator`.
- **Why the coordinator.** A rotation angle is relative to each camera's native sensor orientation. The SDK notes that the front camera's default is 180° on iPads from spring 2024 and 0° before, so a fixed landscape-to-angle table could invert the picture on newer iPads. `videoRotationAngleRelativeToDeviceOrientation(_:)` would map an interface orientation directly, but it needs iOS 27, and the target is iOS 17.
- **`CameraRotationTracker`** (Shared/Tracking): owns one coordinator for the camera behind a connection (and the preview layer). It observes the horizon-level preview and capture angles, reporting changes on the main queue, and applies an angle only when it is supported and different.
- **Previews.** `TMBodyCameraPreview` and `TMCameraPreviewView` (hand) apply the preview angle on layout and on each change, still only in landscape. The body preview still reports `onOrientation` from the interface orientation. Mirroring and the Mac Catalyst paths are unchanged.
- **Hand frames.** `HandCameraService.orient(_:)` became `rotateCapture(to:)`. The hand preview sends the capture angle whenever it changes, and a change starts a new frame generation as before. `configure()` no longer forces `.landscapeLeft` before the preview reports.
- **Body frames.** `BodyTrackingService.configureIfNeeded()` reads the selected camera's horizon-level capture angle from a coordinator on the capture queue and applies it, in place of `videoOrientation = orientation`. It still keeps the semantic, mounting-aware rotation chosen in *fix balancing camera orientation* (2026-11-04), where a fixed angle had inverted front-camera frames. The session is still rebuilt for each interface orientation through `configure(landscapeRight:)`, so one reading per build is current. Apple's AVCam sample also creates and reads the coordinator off the main thread. `orientation` is kept only to detect interface changes.

Verification: XcodeGen, then clean Debug and Release Simulator builds with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0, with no compiler warnings. Only the build tool's "no AppIntents.framework" notice remains. The app launches on the iPad Simulator, which has no camera. Pending on the M1 iPad: hand and body previews upright and mirrored in both landscape orientations, including rotating during a session; hand and body tracking geometry unchanged (fingertip circles, opposition contact, towel workspace, body bubbles and arrows on the joints, Balancing and sit to stand); frames after rotation not scored with the old orientation. If available, a spring 2024 or newer iPad for the 180° front-camera default.

## Sit to stand hip indicator (2026-11-19)

Sit to stand drew nothing over the camera. Since *Body exercises without camera overlay* and *Body target bubble restored*, Balancing draws only `TMSessionBubbleOverlay`, keyed on `BalanceOverlay.wrist`; sit to stand passed no wrist and had relied on the removed shoulder–hip–knee chain, so neither its calibration holds nor practice showed anything. At the product owner's request the hip is now its tracked joint:
- **One hip circle.** `SitToStandBalanceEngine` always sets the hip as the overlay's joint and calibration-hold marker, 100 pt across (`sharedBubbleRadius`). Its arc is the seated and standing hold during calibration (unchanged progress), the calibrated rise (0 seated, 1 standing, clamped) during practice, and empty while returning to the seat. The arc is a display of the rise the activity already counts with; acceptance is unchanged.
- **Pop on each repetition.** `TMSessionBubbleOverlay` gained `captureCompletions`: each increase pops the capture circle (needs `holdRadius`). `BalanceSessionView` passes the completed count; the other Balancing exercises show the capture circle only during calibration, before any count, and a reset for the next set does not pop.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0. Pending on the M1 iPad: the hip circle filling in the seated and standing holds, the arc following the rise in practice and the pop on each count, facing either way and in both orientations; Reduce Motion.

## Plan-wide short and long rest (2026-11-19)

At the product owner's request, rest is no longer set per exercise. Settings has two plan-wide values instead: one short rest between every set and one long rest between exercises. This supersedes the per-exercise rest of *One Exercises section in Settings*.
- **Short rest.** `DailyPlanViewModel.shortRestSeconds` (5–120 s, step 5, default 15; UserDefaults `practice.shortRest`) goes to every session the plan opens: the guided `Routine(restSeconds:)`, `BalancePracticeSettings.restSeconds` and `FingerSessionService.prepare(restSeconds:)`. The programs' own saved rests and the old `practice.rest.<id>` values are no longer read, and nothing migrates them, since one exercise's value could not stand for all.
- **Scripted short rest.** The Indonesian arm-raise and thumb-opposition scripts keep their approved three narrated breaths ("tiga kali napas"). `BreathingRest(opensAfterFirstSet:minimumSeconds:)` now adds a silent step after the last exhale, which lasts until the whole rest reaches the short rest, then the closing line. If the short rest is shorter than the script (about 33 s plus narration), the script keeps its own length, because narration is never cut off. During the silent step the screen shows the timed rest's inhale/exhale rhythm, and nothing is spoken.
- **Long rest.** `DailyPlanViewModel.longRestBreaths` (2–6 breaths, default 6; `practice.longRestBreaths`) sets `LongRestBreath`'s count, which is now an instance value (`init(elapsed:count:)`, `duration(count:)`, `narrationBoundary(at:count:)`). It applies between exercises and between the sides of a one-side-per-session item, scripted or not. `TMDailyPlanNextView` reads the count when it opens, so a rest never changes length partway through. The limit of 6 comes from the recordings. "N napas lagi" exists for 2–6 (generic) and 1–5 or 1–6 (scripted prefixes), and the instructions line says "six breaths". With fewer than six, that line is skipped and the recorded remaining-count line announces the count before the first breath. The last breath always says `inhaleLast`/`exhaleLast`, ahead of the fixed third- and fifth-breath lines. No narration was generated.
- **Settings.** The rest stepper is gone from each exercise row. A new "Istirahat" / "Rest" section follows Exercises, with both steppers and a footer that gives the long rest's approximate length. Strings: `settings.restTitle`, `settings.shortRest`, `settings.longRest` and `settings.restHelp %lld` were added; `plan.restSeconds` was removed.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0. A temporary harness over `SessionDurationClock.swift` and `BreathingRest.swift` passed 23 checks: duration, first breath, last breath and last-breath preface for 2–6 breaths; a 15 s short rest keeping the script's ≈33.6 s with the silent step before the closing; and a 90 s short rest lasting 90 s. Pending on the M1 iPad: Settings steppers and persistence; a timed set rest matching the setting in a finger, a guided and a Balancing exercise; the scripted arm-raise and opposition rests with short and long settings (silent stretch, then closing); long rests of 2–5 breaths in both languages, scripted and generic (no "six breaths" line, correct count, ending on the last-breath lines); Stop/Go and pause during both rests.

## Shader breathing circle (2026-11-19)

At the product owner's request, the circle of the short rest between sets (`TMTherapyBreathingRest`) is drawn by a Metal shader, as the long rest's waves are. The former `Circle` with a rust `strokeBorder` changed scale and opacity once per rest sample with a linear animation (1 s for Balancing and the timed rests, 0.15 s for the scripted breathing), so it stepped and stood still between samples and looked flat.
- **`TMTherapyBreathingCircle`** (DesignSystem) renders `BreathingCircle.metal` (`colorEffect`) inside `TimelineView(.animation)` capped at 60 fps. The breath expansion (0 at rest, 1 at a full breath, from the existing intensity) is an animatable shader argument eased linearly toward each sample over the caller's `animationDuration` (default now 0.25 s, slightly longer than the scripted rest's 0.2 s ticks).
- **Look.** The previous sizes and curves are kept: the circle grows from 76% to full size and the ring (36% of the radius, as before) goes from 25% to full strength. Added: soft feathered edges, an inner-edge highlight, brightening up to 10% toward white and a faint rust halo that swells with the breath, a soft `planPanel` fill lighter at the centre, and an edge wobble of about 1–2% of the radius. The layer leaves room for the halo, so the frame grew from 400 to 460 pt for about the same circle size.
- **Pause.** `TMTherapyBreathingRest(paused:)` stops the wobble clock; the guided, finger and Balancing sessions pass their paused state. The clock is the long rest's, extracted as `ShaderMotionClock` and shared by both shaders.
- **Reduce Motion** draws a still, full-strength circle, as before.
- **Debug.** `-TuntunScreen gallery:rest` shows the short rest breathing on a 6 s inhale and 3 s exhale.

Rest timing, `BreathingRest`, `RestBreath` and narration are unchanged.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0, no warnings. On the iPad Pro 13-inch Simulator, `gallery:rest` drew the circle and it changed between captures, but the system camera prompt covered it, so the look was not fully checked. Pending on the M1 iPad: smoothness at 60 fps and thermals during a rest, the look in light and dark mode, pause freezing the wobble, Reduce Motion, and the scripted arm-raise and opposition rests, the timed guided and finger rests and the Balancing rest.

## Native-English narration voice (2026-11-19)

At the product owner's request, English narration no longer uses Marizka, whose English carried an Indonesian accent. English instructions and English numbers are now spoken by Katherine ("Katherine – Warm & Calm" from the ElevenLabs Voice Library, added to My Voices as "Tuntun EN Katherine"), a native American-English voice. Indonesian stays Marizka. This supersedes the voice named in *English recorded instructions and Marizka-only counts* and *Bilingual Marizka numbers*. The recipe and the calm gate are unchanged.

- **Choice.** The library search for calm, soft female English voices returned 314. The product owner narrowed them to American and middle-aged voices. Free previews of 12 of these were measured with `Tools/measure_pitch.swift`, and the 5 closest to the 163 Hz reference recorded the same 7 lines (5 instructions, countdown "Three", repetition "Seven.") with the production recipe, best of 3 seeds:
  - Katherine: median 135 Hz, 7/7 calm;
  - Deedee: 127 Hz, 7/7;
  - Shanny: 163 Hz, 7/7;
  - Avery: 155 Hz, 6/7;
  - Andrea: 182 Hz, 4/7;
  - Marizka, for comparison: 171 Hz, 6/7.

  The product owner chose Katherine by ear.
- **Generators.** `generate_narration.VOICE_NAMES` maps each language to its voice, and `generate_marizka_counts.py` looks up one voice per language through it, so one run records each number in its language's voice. `--voice-name` and `--voice-id` still override. The `marizka-<language>-…` filenames and the script name are kept, because `NarrationClips.countResourceName` and AGENTS.md depend on them.
- **Scoped runs.** A plain Indonesian `generate_narration.py` run, meant to fill 6 missing lines, re-recorded 135 lines that had no cached raw take or a stale fingerprint. It then re-levelled the whole raw folder and rewrote 308 approved NarrationID clips. Everything was restored from git before anything was committed. `generate_narration.py` gained `--only` (names or globs). With `--only`, both generators level only the selected takes, from a temporary folder, so other bundled clips are never re-encoded. API requests now also retry read timeouts.
- **Indonesian gaps.** `demo.step.nextFinger`, `demo.step.otherArm`, `demo.step.peak`, `demo.step.start`, `session.switchArm.left` and `session.switchArm.right` were recorded in Marizka, so no Indonesian instruction falls back to the system voice.
- **Configurable counts.** Sets (1–5) and repetitions (1–20) are configurable, so English has no count-bearing sentence. Every count is a number clip: repetitions 1–100 cover the 5 × 20 maximum, plus countdowns 1–10.

Result: 187 English instruction clips (median 148 Hz, 90th percentile 162 Hz) and 110 English numbers (median 139 Hz, 90th percentile 153 Hz). All passed `--check`, with no missing, stray or not-calm clips and every countdown within 0.90 s. The Indonesian numbers and instructions are unchanged except for the 6 new clips.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0 passed. The built app contains 456 Indonesian, 187 English and 220 number clips, and sampled clips match the sources byte for byte. Pending on the M1 iPad:
- Katherine's tone and loudness next to Marizka through the speaker;
- English pronunciation and countdown fit at the one-second tick;
- English card timing with the new recordings;
- Stop/Go recognition during English narration, including self-trigger from lines containing "go".

## Narration registry (2026-11-19)

At the product owner's request, narration gets one place to see and adjust every narrated line. Some lines were shown on screen and others were voice-only, and nothing recorded which. The narrated keys were spread across Python constants (`BODY_KEYS`, `FINGER_PREFIXES`, `FINGER_KEYS`, `FORMAT_KEYS`, `SPELLED_FORMAT_KEYS`, `ENGLISH_GROUPS`), prefix scans in the arm-raise wrappers and two copies of the opposition text (`Tools/opposition-left/right-narration.json`). Voice tuning sat in `RETAKE_SEEDS`, `SLOW_LINES` and `LEAD_INS`.
- **Registry.** `Tools/narration-registry.json` has one line per key (438): `group`, `languages`, `display` (`screen`, `partly` with a `shownOn` note, or `voice`), `arguments` and `spellNumber` for format keys, and `tuning.<language>` (`seed`, `speed`, `leadIn`). It holds no wording; the String Catalog stays the only text source, because the app needs both the screen text and `speak.<key>` at runtime for the system-voice fallback. The opposition JSON copies are removed; they matched the catalog exactly.
- **Generator.** `generate_narration.py` builds its lines from the registry and checks it on every run: unknown groups or display values, duplicate keys, arguments on non-format keys, and missing catalog text fail; a `speak.<key>` without a registry line is warned about. The default groups are `finger,body`. An approved script group records on its own into its existing raw folder and manifest (`GROUP_STORAGE`), so the four wrappers only pass `--groups`. The old built-in `opposition-left` and `arm-raise-left` groups, which would have recorded those scripts into the instruction raw folder, are gone. `--report` writes `Documentation/NarrationScript.md`: per group, each key's display, Indonesian and English wording (screen and spoken when they differ) and whether its clips are bundled.
- **Display audit.** Flags come from reading the views as they are today. *Screen*: the guided calibration cards over the camera (`session.opening.*`, `parity.reachOpening`, `towel.opening.*`, `towel.demo`), breathing-rest and long-rest cues (also the scripted `rest.*` and `longRest.*` lines), the long-rest counter, countdown titles (`calibration.countdown`, `*.countdown.next`), the arm-raise set summary, the camera-wait notice, finger demonstration titles, exercise titles, and two finger pause-dialog buttons. *Partly*: `calibration.intro` (card in guided sessions, voice in finger and Balancing) and `session.switchArm.*` (card before an alternating reach, voice during practice). Everything else is voice-only, as the camera caption was removed (*No instruction caption on the camera*); the legacy dark layout (guided sit to stand) was not counted.
- **Unchanged.** Clip names, raw folders, manifests, voice settings, wording and every recording. No Swift changed.

Verification: a temporary harness compared HEAD's line builders (the main generator and all four wrappers) with the new registry path for every group and both languages. All 642 lines had identical name, text, seed, voice settings and lead-in, the fingerprint's inputs, so no cached take is re-recorded. `--dry-run` lists the same counts per group (187 per language, 73, 90, 53, 52). `--check --language en` reports no missing, stray or not-calm clips, as on HEAD. `--report` shows every key bundled. XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0. No device check is needed: nothing at runtime changed.

## Start skips the plan intro narration (2026-11-20)

At the product owner's request, Mulai Latihan on a daily-plan intro is enabled while the intro is read, so a patient who already knows the brief can start at once. This supersedes "Mulai Latihan stays disabled while the intro plays" in *Narrated plan intros*.

- Tapping Start while the narration plays stops it and opens the session the way its end would: `plan.launchAfterRest()` for an intro opened after a rest, otherwise `plan.startCurrent(readiness:)`, which still validates readiness. Stopping clears the narration's completion, so the session never opens twice.
- Start is disabled only while that readiness check runs. While the intro is paused it still reads "Lanjutkan" and resumes.
- The debug "Start without narration" button now calls the same path (`DailyPlanIntroViewModel.skipNarration()`).

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build. Pending on the M1 iPad: Start during each intro (scripted and unscripted, both languages) cuts the speech at once and opens the session; an untouched intro still opens on its own; backgrounding during the intro still pauses it, and Lanjutkan resumes.

## Whole-hand circle follows the grip (2026-11-20)

In Kepal dan Buka Tangan (full fist) the whole-hand circle did not respond to the hand and looked jagged. It was deliberately fixed at 2.3 palm lengths (*Smooth whole-hand circle*), so its only size changes were wrist-to-knuckle foreshortening noise, and its centre and size jumped once per 30 Hz camera frame because `TMTherapyMergingGuide` animated only `merge`. It now grows as the hand opens and shrinks as it closes, and moves smoothly. This supersedes the "stays still while the hand opens and closes" rule for full and hook fist. Forearm rotation keeps one size.
- **Openness.** `FingerActivity.gripOpenness` (full and hook fist; presentation only, never scored) maps the same filtered grip metric that scoring uses onto the patient's calibrated start (open) and target (fist) poses, so a small range still swings the circle fully. Before calibration it uses a nominal 0.10 (open) to 0.50 (fist) range. It updates in every phase, including calibration and the countdown, keeps its last value on a frame without a metric, and clears on recalibration.
- **Size.** `TMFingerLandmarkOverlay(openness:)` sizes the circle from 1.3 palm lengths (closed) to 2.3 (open), within the existing 100 pt minimum, 75% viewport maximum and on-screen clamp. The bounding-box fallback is no longer rounded to 4 pt steps.
- **Glide.** `TMTherapyMergingGuide` gained `glide` for a single circle: a private animatable layer interpolates its centre and diameter, outside the merging layer's own animatable data, so thumb opposition, fold towel and body bubbles are unchanged. The whole-hand circle (all three exercises) passes a 0.15 s spring without bounce; Reduce Motion places it at once.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0. Pending on the M1 iPad: in full and hook fist the circle grows on opening and shrinks on closing through calibration and practice; a small-range hand still shows a clear change; the motion is smooth without visible stepping or lag; the circle stays inside the preview near the edges; the arc, pop and acknowledgment at the fist and open-hand holds; Reduce Motion; forearm rotation keeps one size; the overlay keeps 30 fps. Tune the nominal range and the 1.3 closed ratio on the device.

## Recorded intro purposes, demo card and Balancing tracking lines (2026-11-20)

At the product owner's request, every narrated line that still fell back to the system voice is now recorded. All registry keys already had clips. The gaps were keys the app speaks that had never been added to the registry:

- **`plan.purpose.<item>`** (15 keys). These are read on each plan intro opened from Beranda. Indonesian used the system voice for every exercise without a script, and for opposition and arm raise when their script does not apply. English used it for all 15.
- **`parity.example.left` / `parity.example.right`**. This is the arm raise and elbow bend demonstration card.
- **Balancing tracking-loss lines**: `balance.tracking`, `balance.pelvisMissing`, `balance.activeWristMissing`, `rotation.tracking` and `rotation.armOutOfView`. `BalanceSessionViewModel` speaks the current instruction when its practice countdown ends. That instruction is one of these keys when the body, wrist or arm is lost at that moment. Shoulder flexion and internal rotation use the `rotation.*` keys through `BalanceExercise.key(_:)`.

The 22 keys were added to `Tools/narration-registry.json` in the `body` group for both languages:
- the purposes and the demo card as `screen`;
- the tracking lines as `voice`.

They were recorded with `--only` (Marizka, and Katherine taking the calmest of three seeds), so no approved clip was re-recorded or re-levelled. The wording is unchanged in the String Catalog.

Lines that ran faster than the approved clips were retaken with registry tuning. The pace baseline is the approved lines over 40 characters: Indonesian median 11.4 characters per second (90th percentile 13.3), English median 14.1 (90th percentile 17.2).
- Indonesian `balance.activeWristMissing`: speed 0.78.
- Indonesian `plan.purpose.sitToStand`: speed 0.8, seed 7107. The first retake measured 184 Hz.
- English `plan.purpose.directionalReach`, `elbowBend`, `forearmRotation`, `hookFist` and `internalRotation`: speed 0.8.

What still uses the system voice: only failure paths, when a count clip is missing or fails to play. `TherapySessionViewModel`'s numeric-key branch also would, but nothing reaches it today. `narration-id-recovery.sharing.m4a` is a stray clip with no registry line or catalog key. It is left in place.

Verification:
- `--check --language en`: no missing, stray or not-calm clips; English pitch median 148 Hz.
- `--report`: all 460 keys bundled.
- XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0 passed.
- The built app contains 478 Indonesian and 209 English narration clips, and sampled clips match their sources byte for byte.

Pending on the M1 iPad:
- each plan intro reads its purpose recorded in both languages, and Start still cuts it;
- the arm raise and elbow bend demo card plays recorded;
- tone and loudness match the neighbouring lines;
- Stop/Go recognition still works during the new lines.

## No calibration card on the camera (2026-11-20)

At the user's request no exercise shows instruction text over the camera any more. *No instruction caption on the camera* kept the guided calibration card: white measured text on black along the video's bottom edge during the opening and demonstration cues of arm raise, elbow bend, directional reach and fold towel. It is removed; narration speaks those cues. This supersedes "the guided calibration card stays" in that entry.

- **Views.** `TherapyLightSession` drops `calibrationCard` and its overlay; `showsCalibrationCard` is renamed `showsCameraOpening` and still keeps the calibration layout (heading, hidden counters) during those cues. `lightGuidanceText` loses its `compact` option and `TMTherapySessionView.calibrationCaptionSize` is removed.
- **Engine.** That card hosted the measured-reading gate, so `SessionEngine.announce` now opens a cue's `GuidanceGate` already read when no card shows it (`cueShownOnCard`: false for the four light-layout kinds). Their opening and demonstration cues advance after narration completes, fails or reaches its watchdog, then the usual quiet delay or demonstration hold. Dark-layout cards still wait for reading.
- **Design system.** `TMTherapyVideoFooter` and `TMMeasuredInstructionText`'s single-line `compact` mode had no other caller and are removed.
- **Narration registry.** The 17 lines flagged as shown on that card (`calibration.intro`, `parity.example.*`, `parity.reachOpening`, `session.opening.armRaise/elbowBend.*`, `session.switchArm.*`, `towel.opening.*`, `towel.demo`) are now `voice`; `Documentation/NarrationScript.md` regenerated. No wording or recording changed.
- **Unchanged.** Narration, quiet delays, the demonstration column and title, counters, controls, camera-wait and failure notices, countdown cover and breathing rest.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0 succeed; no references remain to the removed card, footer or caption size. Pending on the M1 iPad: no text over the camera in arm raise, elbow bend, reach and fold towel (both languages); their opening and demonstration cues advance on their own after narration, also with VoiceOver on and when narration fails; finger and Balancing sessions unchanged.

## Indicator visible during narration (2026-11-20)

The user reported that in some body exercises the tracking indicator disappeared when narration started, which also hid the acceptance pop. Causes in the guided session (arm raise, elbow bend, directional reach):

- `SessionEngine.observation` dropped every frame outside `.activity` and while the countdown cue played, so the opening cues, "try the movement", "calibration complete" and the countdown cue froze or hid the indicator; `TherapySessionView` also drew it only in `.activity`.
- `MovementActivity` cleared its guide on each calibration capture, on entering the countdown, during the countdown, and (Indonesian arm-raise script) on every accepted start or target, exactly when the praise or next line starts. Clearing unmounted `TMSessionBubbleOverlay`, so its pop never played. Reach cleared its guide on every accepted arrival the same way. The countdown therefore also contradicted §5 ("the target is drawn quietly beneath it").

Changes:
- **Activities.** New `ActivityEvent.preview(TrackingObservation)`: it moves only the drawn guide, credits nothing, changes no phase and returns no facts. `MovementActivity` rebuilds its capture or practice guide from it (frozen hold progress, none after an acceptance); `DirectionalReachActivity` moves the wrist on its kept bubbles (or the start bubble on the wrist); towel and timed practice ignore it; `AlternatingSidesActivity` forwards it to the active side. A dropout keeps the last guide for 0.5 s. Accepted holds and arrivals keep their guide at full progress until the next frame, the movement countdown draws the quiet start and target bubbles with the live joint, and each completed capture increments `MovementSnapshot.captureCompletions`.
- **Engine.** `previewWhileGated` sends gated frames (opening stages, or a countdown waiting for its cue) to the activity's `preview` and updates only the presentation. Scoring gates are unchanged.
- **View.** `TherapySessionView` draws the bubble overlay during the activity and the opening, instruction and demonstration stages (not while paused, waiting for the camera, in feedback, rest or summary). It passes `captureCompletions` and gives captures no bubble dwell, so a capture pops its circle once.
- **Unchanged.** Balancing already previewed gated frames (`showSkeletonWhileGated`); finger and fold-towel indicators.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0 succeed. Pending on the M1 iPad: the Indonesian arm-raise script keeps the joint drawn and pops on every accepted start and target while its lines play; English arm raise, elbow bend and reach keep the indicator through the opening cues, "try the movement", "calibration complete" and the countdown cue, and calibration captures pop once; no hold or repetition is credited during a gated cue; alternating sides hand over without a stale guide.

## Completion pulse replaces droplet burst (2026-11-20)

The hold-completion "pop" sent eight droplets flying past the guide circle, which read as circles shooting around the indicator. The user asked for a pulse instead, drawn with a Metal shader like the indicator itself.

- **Shader.** `completionPulse` (DesignSystem/Components/CompletionPulse.metal) draws a soft glow in the tint that swells out from the circle's edge and fades, twice like a heartbeat: the first beat over the first 60% of the pulse, a weaker second beat (60% strength, less travel) overlapping from 35%. The outline brightens slightly toward white during the first beat. Beats travel up to 35% of the radius and widen as they go.
- **View.** `TMTherapyPopBurst` keeps its API (`trigger`, `diameter`, `tint`) and callers, so `TMTherapyGuideBubble`, `TMTherapyMergingGuide(popsOnCompletion:)` and `TMSessionBubbleOverlay` are unchanged. The pulse lasts 0.7 s (formerly 0.5 s); the shader layer is padded so the glow is never clipped. Reduce Motion plays one 0.3 s beat with little travel.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build succeed. Pending on the M1 iPad: the pulse on finger holds, body bubble acceptance, calibration captures and sit-to-stand counts; visibility on bright video; Reduce Motion; the overlay keeps 30 fps.

## Loading overlay around sessions (2026-11-20)

The user noticed that before and after a therapy session the UI did not update at once: some work already ran in the background, but the rest stalled the screen. Starting runs `SessionAudioService.prepare()` (about 33 audio clips loaded synchronously) and voice listening on the main actor, from the intro's Start and again in the guided session's `start()`; the finger session configures its guidance audio and listening, Balancing prepares audio. Ending tears down speech, listening, music and capture on the main actor while the plan cover changes page.

- **Design system.** New `TMLoadingOverlay` (spinner and one message) over `TMFrostedBackdrop`, the countdown's frosted backdrop, now shared with `TMPracticeCountdown`. `presentationDelay` (50 ms) lets a caller get the overlay drawn before blocking work.
- **Before.** `TherapyReadinessViewModel.performAction()` raises `preparingSession`, waits, then evaluates and starts listening; on success the flag stays raised while the session opens. The guided view shows the overlay while `isStarting`; finger and Balancing view models add `isPreparing`, cleared once audio and capture are started (before the finger checkpoint save). Each `start()` waits `presentationDelay` before its first synchronous work.
- **After.** Session view models add `isFinishing`. Guided: every Stop goes through `end()` (overlay until the summary is shown and saved), and leaving the summary or the entry failure page through `leave()`, which runs `exit()` before navigating; `exit()` now runs once. Finger: the pause dialog's End goes through `end()`, and the all-done page shows the overlay while saving instead of the inline spinner. Balancing: `leave()` awaits `end()`, and `stop()` runs once (it is still called on disappear).
- **Strings.** `loading.preparingSession`, `loading.finishingSession` (screen only, not narrated).
- **Follow-up.** Moving `SessionAudioService.prepare()` off the main actor would remove the stall itself; not done here.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0 succeed. Pending on the M1 iPad: the overlay appears at once on the intro's Start and stays until the session is live (guided, finger, Balancing; both languages); End and the summary's way on show it until the next page; the finger all-done page while saving; Reduce Motion (no fade) and VoiceOver reading the message; no double teardown or lost save.

## Audio session off the main thread (2026-11-20)

The console repeated `AVAudioSession_iOS.mm:978 This method can lead to UI unresponsiveness if called on the main thread`. `NarrationAudioSession.activate` ran `setCategory`/`setActive(true)` synchronously from main-actor callers: the plan intro and long rest (`SessionAudioService.prepareRestNarration`), music (`startMusic`, `resumeMusic` in both audio services), finger-session setup (`GuidanceAudioService.configure`, `enableVoice`) and every first button click after `GuidanceAudioService.stop()` had deactivated the session.

- **Session.** `NarrationAudioSession.activate()` is now `async`: the request is counted on the caller (so the late-deactivation token still works) and the configuration and activation run on one serial `sessionQueue`, which also runs `deactivateInBackground()`, so the two keep their order. `activateBlocking()` keeps the synchronous form for `AudioInputWorker`, which already runs on its own queue; it asserts it is not on the main thread.
- **Callers.** The plan intro awaits `prepareRestNarration()` before its first line; the long rest already awaited in its task. `configure(language:)` and `enableVoice()` are `async` and awaited in the finger session's async start, retry and voice paths. Music starts from a task that awaits activation; pausing, stopping, suspension and interruptions cancel it so music cannot start after a stop. A button click plays at once while the session is active; the first click after a deactivation waits for activation.
- **Not used.** `AVAudioSession.activate(options:completionHandler:)`/`deactivate(options:completionHandler:)` exist only from iOS 27 (iOS 17 is the deployment target), and `setCategory` still blocks; the serial queue covers every supported version with one path.
- **Strings (same change).** Reach summary outcome and phase names used an interpolated `LocalizedStringKey`, which looked up `session.reachOutcome.%@`/`session.reachPhase.%@` and showed raw keys; they now use literal keys. Number-only accessibility values (`TMTherapyProgressDots`, `TMTherapyStepStrip`) are verbatim, the per-repetition chart's axis labels are `chart.axis.repetition`/`chart.axis.value`, and `TMTherapyPageHeader` takes an optional title instead of an empty key. Unused stale catalog keys were removed; keys built at runtime (and every narrated key) are `manual` in the String Catalog so Xcode does not mark them stale.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0 succeed without warnings; every key the compiler extracts is in the catalog.
