Pending on the M1 iPad: no main-thread warning in the console through the plan intro, long rest, a guided, finger and Balancing session and button taps after a finger session; narration and music start without clipping or a late start after Stop; the first button click after a session; Stop/Go during narration; the reach summary shows translated outcomes and phases in both languages.

## Sit to stand repetition arc (2026-11-20)

At the product owner's request, sit to stand's hip indicator now shows whether a repetition is progressing. Since *Sit to stand hip indicator*, the arc during practice was the calibrated rise alone. The required forward lean drew nothing. A stand without the lean filled the arc although it would never count. The arc emptied while sitting back down, so the pop came on an empty circle. Waiting to return to the seat looked the same as live practice. The 100 pt circle, the size of the arm exercises' target bubbles, also read as a place to reach rather than the tracked hip.
- **One cycle per repetition.** `SitToStandActivity` publishes `repetitionProgress`. The lean toward its target fills 0–¼, rising from `liftRise` to `standingRise` fills ¼–¾, standing holds ¾, and lowering from `standingRise` to `seatedRise` fills the rest. The arc is full during the 0.25 s seated dwell, just before the count and its pop, and restarts after. It only moves back when the movement does, for example when sitting down from a partial rise. Counting rules are unchanged.
- **Quiet when it won't count.** `repetitionCounts` is false once a repetition is flagged (`sts.leanFirst`, `sts.slowDown`) and while waiting for the seat. `BalanceOverlay.holdActive` carries it, also false during the return to the seat before practice, to `TMSessionBubbleOverlay(captureActive:)`, which draws the hip as a quiet outline with no arc.
- **72 pt hip circle.** `SitToStandBalanceEngine.hipDiameter` uses `fixedBubbleRadius`; the pop follows the same radius.
- **Step arrow.** The existing blue calibration cue (`SessionMovementCue`, drawn by `TMSessionGuideArrows`) shows the next move. It is fixed to the calibrated poses, and the camera stays still during a session. While leaning, it is the trunk turning forward around the seated hip from the seated shoulder to the lean target, and the shoulder moves along it. While rising, it runs from the seated hip to the standing hip. During the trial, before a stand has been seen, it points one trunk length up. While sitting down or returning to the seat, it runs back down. There is no arrow during the holds, the countdown, standing still, an invalid repetition or lost tracking. `BalanceOverlay` gained `calibrationJoint` and `calibrationPivot`. `BalanceSessionView` draws the cue with the hip marker only when `calibrationJoint` is set, so the arm exercises' wrist hold stays free of their range cue as before.

No narration or strings changed: `sts.leanForward`, `sts.standUp`, `sts.sitDown` and `sts.leanFirst` already speak each step.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0. A temporary harness over `SitToStandActivity.swift` drove synthetic side-view poses through calibration and practice and passed 14 checks:
- the arc went 0→0.25 leaning, 0.25→0.75 rising, held 0.75 standing (with no arrow), went 0.75→1.00 lowering, was full just before the count and restarted after it, never going backward
- a stand without the lean was quiet, empty, arrowless and not counted, and the next repetition was live again
- the trial showed the up arrow and then, after the stand hold, the down arrow

Pending on the M1 iPad: the arc filling during the lean and while sitting down; a full arc lining up with the pop and the spoken count; the quiet outline after a stand without the lean and while returning to the seat; the 72 pt circle reading as on the hip; the lean arrow bending the right way facing left and right, in both orientations; the up and down arrows matching the real hip path, with the passed stretch dimming; the arrow switching between steps without a jarring jump; no arrow during holds and the countdown; Reduce Motion.

## Sit to stand calibration and milestone pops (2026-11-20)

The product owner still found sit to stand unclear after *Sit to stand repetition arc*. They asked for one repeated pattern: sit, stand, sit, with the indicator popping each time a position is reached. They also chose to drop the forward-lean rule and to play the `hold-pop` sound on the new pops.
- **Calibration order.** The seated hold (`sts.holdSeated`, 3 s) now pops the hip circle. The trial then asks "Stand and hold" (`sts.holdStanding`) from its start, under an up arrow: one trunk length up until a stand is seen, then to the best stand so far. The arrow hides while the stand hold fills. The stand hold is now required. Completing it pops the circle and asks the patient to sit (`sts.sitDown`) under a down arrow, and calibration finishes once they have settled back into the seat. Sitting before the hold completes asks them to stand again; this supersedes "sitting early still finishes the trial".
- **No lean rule.** Practice stages are seated → rising → standing → lowering. The `.leaning` stage, `leanReached`, the trial's peak lean, the lean targets and the `sts.leanFirst` flag are gone, so any controlled stand and sit counts. This supersedes the lean requirement of the original activity and of *Sit to stand without the ankle*. Trunk lean is still recorded. The abrupt-descent rule (`sts.slowDown`) stays.
- **Two halves, two pops.** `repetitionProgress` fills 0→1 with the rise to `standingRise`. Reaching the stand pops it and switches to "sit down" in the same frame. It then fills 0→1 again as the patient sits, and the count pops it. This supersedes the one-cycle arc and the lean arrow of *Sit to stand repetition arc*. The quiet outline for a repetition that cannot count, the 72 pt size and the up/down arrows stay.
- **One pop trigger.** `SitToStandSnapshot.completions` counts every milestone: the held seat, the held stand, each practice stand and each count. It only grows. `BalanceOverlay.completions` carries it, and `BalanceSessionView` passes it as `captureCompletions`, falling back to completed repetitions for the other exercises. A count therefore pops once.
- **Sound.** `SessionAudioCue.holdPopped` plays `hold-pop.wav` (`HoldPopSound.resourceName`, shared with the finger sessions' `GuidanceAudioService`), alone or as a guidance prefix before a line. When the milestone count rises without a repetition, `BalanceSessionViewModel.updateSitToStandAudio` plays the pop, then the instruction that changed with it. A count keeps the target chime and `voice.returnSameSide`.
- **Narration retired.** `sts.trial`, `sts.leanForward` and `sts.leanFirst` were removed from the String Catalog, the registry and the manifests, with their bundled clips and raw takes. `NarrationScript.md` was refreshed with `--report`. Nothing was recorded. `sts.standUp`'s second line ("Keep leaning forward as you rise") remains as advice.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0. A temporary harness over `SitToStandActivity.swift` drove synthetic side-view poses and passed 18 checks:
- the seated hold popped and asked for a stand; there was no pop while rising
- sitting before the stand hold asked for a stand again, under an up arrow
- the stand hold popped, switched to sit down and showed a down arrow
- calibration ended in the countdown, with no arrow
- practice started under an up arrow
- two straight-up repetitions with no lean each counted with two pops, each arc full at its pop, the sit-down instruction on the stand pop's frame, and each half's arc only growing

Pending on the M1 iPad:
- the calibration order and its narration in both languages
- the pops and `hold-pop` at the held seat, the held stand and each stand, and a single pop with the chime on each count
- the arrows facing either way and in both orientations
- a stand without the lean counting
- an abrupt sit not counting
- Stop/Go during the pops
- Reduce Motion

## Foundation color palette (2026-11-20)

The product owner supplied the Figma "Foundation Colors" page (Primary Yellow BG/Normal/Pressed, Secondary Blue, Error and Success Normal/Pressed, and the Background, Main Dark, Lighter Components and Disabled neutrals) for the new iteration of Beranda, the long and short rests and Progres. `TherapyPalette` now holds these as private raw tokens and maps its semantic tokens onto them, so call sites keep their token names. This supersedes the rust `brand` of *Daily plan timeline* and the system green, red and blue in the monochrome palette.

- **Brand is yellow, labels on it are dark.** `brand` is #FFB700. White on it is about 1.8:1, so `onBrand` is Main Dark (#1A1A1A, about 10.5:1), and the completion mark takes a `checkColor` (`onBrand` on yellow).
- **No yellow text.** Even Pressed #E28C00 is about 3.0:1 on white. The product owner chose Main Dark for text that the design shows in the brand colour. The new `brandInk` token is used by the target and progress numbers, "‹ Kembali", the movement-history link and the report's disclosure tint. Icons, rings, markers, outlines and fills stay yellow.
- **Long rest.** The waves are yellow (`brand`, two lighter yellows, `brandPressed` in front), so their cue, counter and buttons use `onBrand` instead of white.
- **Status colours.** `success`/`setDone` use Success/Normal. `green`, which also carries text and white labels, uses Success/Pressed (about 7:1). `warning` and the new `destructive` use Error/Normal (about 5:1).
- **Neutrals.** `canvas` is #F9F9F9, `action` is Main Dark and `actionMuted` is Disabled in light mode. Dark mode keeps the earlier values where Figma gives none.
- **Blue.** `guidanceBlue` and `restRing` use Secondary Blue #58A7F8.
- **Unchanged.** The session screen's orange failure text stays `.orange`, because the new red would lose contrast on the dark session panels.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build (iPad Pro 13-inch, M5) succeeded. Contrast figures were computed from the hex values. Pending on the baseline iPad: Home timeline and the "Lanjutkan Latihan" pill, the exercise intro and live counters, the long and short rests, Progress and the session report, the pause dialog and a destructive confirmation, in light and dark mode.

## Exercise detail panel (2026-11-23)

The Figma for the day page adds a patient-facing detail panel per exercise, floating over the list on a blurred backdrop like an App Store detail card on iPad. It replaces the push to the finger or session report from the day page's cards; those pages remain for now but are unlinked from the day page.

- **Presentation.** `HistoryRouter.exerciseDetail` holds the open panel; `PracticeDayViewModel.open` sets it and `ReportsViewModel` forwards it to the stack owner, which overlays `TMDetailPanel` on the whole `NavigationStack`, navigation bar included, and fades it in and out. Popping clears it. It is an overlay rather than a sheet so the page behind stays visible through the frost.
- **Figures.** `ExerciseProgress` (History/Models) joins every guided, Balancing and finger session of the exercise. The day's best repetition is the highest peak angle, or the best share of the calibrated range for finger exercises (from their key repetitions, as finger summaries keep no per-repetition metrics), or the repetition count when there is no measure (fold towel). Average seconds per repetition come from every repetition's duration for guided sessions and from practice time for finger sessions.
- **Baseline.** At the product owner's choice the menu offers the first session, the previous session, or the latest session on or before the same day a week earlier. With none, the panel says today is the starting point. Changes within 5° (or 5% of range) read as steady; `SessionReportViewModel` now takes its thresholds from `ExerciseProgress` so the two never disagree.
- **Picture.** No per-exercise illustration exists, so the comparison draws the best key repetition of each session as a skeleton (`TMPoseComparison`). The pose shown is the frame that differs most from the repetition's first frame (`KeyRep.peakFrame`), a display choice and not a scoring rule. Poses are aligned at the neck (wrist for hands).
- **Record.** "Catatan latihan" shows the day's hardest key repetition (the recorder's lowest peak; across sets the lowest, then the longest), its clip when saved, otherwise its skeleton, with its duration and its peak against the baseline's best.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build (`IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0`) succeeded. Pending: the panel on the baseline iPad in both orientations, with Reduce Motion, large text and VoiceOver (the backdrop and close circle dismiss it), and with real history for an arm exercise, a finger exercise and fold towel. The baseline menu's Secondary Blue text (about 2.6:1 on white) follows the Figma and needs a contrast decision.

## Exercise detail insights (2026-11-23)

At the product owner's request, "Perkembangan gerakanmu" gains insight rows (`TMInsightRow`) under the legend, from data already saved, through `ExerciseProgress.insights(against:)`:

- **Pace.** The day's mean seconds per repetition against the baseline's, averaged the same way on both sides; within 0.5 s it reads as the same. It is worded as quicker or slower, never better or worse, because a quicker repetition is not necessarily better therapy.
- **Consistency.** The best minus the lowest peak across the day's repetitions (at least three); within 5° (or 5% of range) every repetition reads as steady.
- **Stamina.** The day's last repetition peak against its first, in practice order across sessions and sets. A drop is worded gently ("wajar jika mulai lelah") with the yellow attention tone, never red.
- **Volume.** Today's repetitions against the baseline's, with the number of sessions since the baseline.

Pace and volume need a baseline and follow the menu; consistency and stamina describe the day alone. Finger sessions keep only their key repetitions, so their consistency and stamina rest on the first, hardest and last repetition of each set. Fold towel, which has no measure, now shows the section with its pace and volume rows but no picture or legend. The panel title names the side ("Angkat lengan kiri") when every session of the day practised one side.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build (`IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0`) succeeded with no warnings in the changed files. Pending: the rows with real history on the baseline iPad in both languages, at large text sizes and with VoiceOver.

## Exercise detail record choices (2026-11-23)

"Catatan latihan" on the exercise detail panel now offers the day's first, hardest and last key repetition instead of the hardest alone, at the product owner's request. The choice is day-level, matching the panel's day figures: first and last are the earliest and latest repetitions holding that role in practice order (sessions oldest first, then set, then repetition), and the hardest is, across sets, the one that reached least, then the longest. Hardest stays the default. The choice uses `TMChoiceChips` in the panel's yellow style rather than the system segmented control of the clinician report; with one repetition (all three roles) a note replaces the chips. A native switch, "Tampilkan kerangka", swaps a saved clip for its skeleton and appears only when the chosen repetition has a clip. The blue call-out follows the choice: the hardest keeps "Gerakanmu sedikit berbeda di repetisi ke-N", the first and last say "Begini gerakanmu di awal / di akhir, repetisi ke-N"; the duration and baseline-change tiles describe the chosen repetition.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build (`IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0`) succeeded. Pending on the baseline iPad: switching roles and the skeleton switch with real clips, VoiceOver selection state on the chips, and large text.

## Exercise detail wording and trend (2026-11-23)

At the product owner's request the detail panel speaks in the exercise's own words and plainer language for non-medical readers.

- **Exercise wording.** `ExerciseWording` maps each measured exercise and the day's side to a headline lead and a card caption: arm raise "Lengan kirimu bisa terangkat", shoulder flexion "…terangkat ke depan", elbow bend "Siku kirimu bisa menekuk", the rotations "…berputar ke luar / ke dalam", sit to stand "Saat berdiri, badanmu bisa", finger exercises "Jari tangan / Ibu jari / Lengan bawah kirimu bisa bergerak / berputar". "Lenganmu…" is used when sides alternated. Changes read in matching words: lifting is higher or lower, bending and turning further or less far, standing straighter or less straight; ranges keep "% lebih jauh / lebih pendek". Reach, cross midline and fold towel keep the generic text.
- **Trend.** "Hasil terbaik tiap sesi" charts each session's best result up to the shown day, at most ten points, keeping the baseline session even when older, today last (`ExerciseProgress.trend`). Finger sessions gain a value once their key repetitions are read, so the panel reads those of the sessions the chart can show. The insight rows moved under the chart, full width, so the figure and headline keep the reference layout.
- **Record cards.** The heading says "gerakan ke-5" rather than "repetisi", names the set only when the day had several, and reads as a plain sentence for each role. "Lama gerakan" compares the move with the day's average ("Lebih lama dari biasanya (rata-rata 12,5 detik)", within 0.5 s "Sama seperti biasanya"). The second card names the movement ("Seberapa tinggi lenganmu terangkat"), shows the move's own result, then how it compares with the baseline in the exercise's words and with the day's best ("Terbaikmu hari ini: 112°" or "Ini hasil terbaikmu hari ini"). `TMDetailStatTile` now takes several lines.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build (`IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0`) succeeded. Pending on the baseline iPad: wording for each exercise and side in both languages, the chart with real history and a changing baseline, and large text.

## Exercise detail figure and layout (2026-11-23)

A design pass on the detail panel at the product owner's request.

- **One figure style.** Both figures in the panel, the progress comparison and the "Catatan latihan" skeleton replay, are drawn as a filled silhouette (`TMFigureShape`, `TMFigureRenderer`) closer to the reference illustration, for a lower reading load: thick round-capped limbs, a filled torso or palm, a round head, a soft blue-grey body with no joint dots, and only the moving limb in yellow. The comparison draws the earlier pose's moving limb as a pale blue ghost behind it. The clinician report pages keep the stick figure, whose joints a clinician reads.
- **Perkembangan gerakanmu.** The illustration has no border and 24 pt rounded corners. Beside it, one column holds the headline, the since line and the legend, then the insight rows in one card (`TMInsightCard`) with coloured bullets instead of icons. The trend chart stays below at full width. The side-by-side row has an explicit ideal width, because `ViewThatFits` measured the sentences unwrapped and always chose the stacked fallback.
- **Catatan latihan.** The two tiles always share one row; their text wraps inside each tile.
- **Fix.** The headline lead key was built inside a `LocalizedStringKey` string interpolation, which made it a format key and showed the raw key; it is now built as a `String` first.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug Simulator build succeeded, and the panel was rendered with synthetic arm-raise poses through a temporary debug gallery screen (removed) on the iPad Pro 13-inch (M5) Simulator. Pending on the baseline iPad: real recordings for body and hand exercises, both languages, large text.

## Calmer detail-panel figure (2026-11-23)

The product owner asked for a calmer figure in which every joint stays clear. This supersedes the filled silhouette's look in *Exercise detail figure and layout*; the clinician reports still use the stick figure.

- The silhouette becomes a faint backdrop: limbs about 20% thinner (0.30 of the shoulder width, 0.24 of the knuckle span for a hand) at 28% opacity instead of 60%.
- A thin 2.5 pt skeleton in the new `figureLine` slate token runs over it, with a small white dot ringed in slate at every joint, so the joints a reader follows are always visible.

## Delete a key-repetition clip (2026-11-23)

Caregivers asked to remove a single recorded video from history without deleting the whole session.

- The exercise detail panel's practice record shows a destructive "Hapus video" pill under a shown clip. A confirmation dialog states that the skeleton and practice figures are kept; confirming deletes only that repetition's `.mp4`.
- `SessionRepository.deleteRepClip(sessionID:executionID:repNumber:)` and `FingerSessionRepository.deleteRepClip(sessionID:key:)` remove one clip file and do nothing when it is absent. Clip naming now lives in one helper per repository, shared with saving.
- `RecordedRep` carries a `RepClip` (URL plus its repository reference). After a deletion the panel drops the clip from its loaded repetitions, so the record falls back to the skeleton and the switch disappears; a failure shows an error under the clip and is logged.
- The panel lists only clips already moved into place, so a clip still encoding in the background is not offered for deletion until it has landed.
- The unlinked session and finger report pages keep their replay sections unchanged.

## Readable exercise detail figures (2026-11-23)

Readers saw many numbers in the exercise detail panel without knowing what each one meant. An arm-raise panel with history showed about 18, and the day's best appeared four times under four names. Patients and clinicians are equal readers, so every figure stays. The Figma layout and section order are kept; only wording and small in-place additions changed.

- **Stat tiles** use `TMDetailStatTile`'s existing caption-first mode: the caption names the figure ("Sudut tertinggi hari ini", "Lama satu repetisi (rata-rata)", "Waktu bergerak"), and the line under the rule says what it measures. Arm raise and elbow bend say where 0° is ("0° lengan di samping badan, 90° setinggi bahu, 180° lurus ke atas"; elbow bend reports flexion, "0° siku lurus"), taken from `KeyRepRecorder.angle(in:)`. The Balancing engines' own estimates (shoulder flexion, rotations, sit to stand) have no documented zero, so they keep a generic camera-estimate line. Finger ranges say that 100% is the farthest move at calibration; fold towel says that one repetition is four folds.
- **Baseline menu** reads "Dibandingkan dengan: Sesi pertama", using the legend's short names. Its value uses `action` instead of the Figma's Secondary Blue, which resolves the 2.6:1 contrast question from *Exercise detail panel*.
- **Pose picture** has a caption naming both figures ("Biru samar: sesi pertama · Kuning: hari ini"), so colour is not the only cue. The legend says "Hari ini" instead of "Saat ini".
- **Insight rows** lead with their aspect in bold ("Tempo", "Kestabilan", "Daya tahan", "Jumlah") through `TMInsightRow.title`. The pace sentences drop "Tempomu", which the title now names.
- **Trend** has a `TMTrendChart.caption` that says what a rising line means, in the exercise's change style.
- **Practice record** no longer repeats the day's best value ("Terbaikmu hari ini: …"). It keeps "Ini hasil terbaikmu hari ini" when it applies. The call-out's duration caption reads "Lama gerakan ini".

Verification: `xcodegen generate` and a Debug Simulator build with `IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0` (iPad Pro 13-inch (M5)) succeeded. Pending: a visual check against the Figma frame with real history for arm raise, a finger exercise and fold towel, in both languages, with large text and VoiceOver, and on the baseline iPad.


## Roomier trend chart (2026-11-23)

The exercise detail panel's trend chart looked crowded: up to ten points in 160 pt, the current point's value and "Hari ini" marker running into the title, and a date under every point. Its position in the Figma layout is unchanged; only spacing in `TMTrendChart` changed.

- The default height is now 220 pt, and the title block sits 16 pt above the plot instead of 10 pt. In the panel the chart gets 12 pt more space above it.
- The y-domain is explicit: the values' span (at least 10 units, so a flat line isn't stretched) plus 35% above for the markers and 15% below.
- The x-axis labels only the first, last, baseline and current sessions. Every point keeps its date for VoiceOver.

## Coloured insight sentences (2026-11-23)

At the user's request, the exercise detail panel's insight rows colour their sentence as well as the bullet: green when positive, red (`warning`) when negative. This replaces the earlier rule that the tone coloured only the bullet and that a decline was shown in yellow, never red. `TMInsightRow.Tone.attention` is now `.negative`. The bold title ("Tempo:") and the muted detail line keep their colours, and the words still carry the meaning, so colour is never the only cue.

- Pace stays neutral, because quicker is not always better in therapy.
- Consistency: even is green, varied is red.

## Delete an exercise's day record (2026-11-23)

Users asked for a way to delete an exercise's record, for example a session recorded by mistake. They chose to delete one exercise on one day, and to remove its plan mark too.

- The exercise detail panel ends with a destructive "Hapus catatan latihan ini" pill under the disclaimer, which keeps the Figma sections unchanged. A confirmation dialog names the exercise and the day and says the deletion can't be undone.
- `ExerciseDetailViewModel.deleteRecord()` deletes every session in `progress.sessionsOnDay` through `SessionRepository.delete(id:)` or `FingerSessionRepository.delete(_:)`. Each call removes the summary, its replays and its clips.
- **Plan mark.** `PracticeHistory` adds plan-completed items to a day, so the deleted exercise would otherwise stay on the day page. `DailyPlanHistoryRepository` gains `removeCompleted(_:on:)`, and `LocalDailyPlanProgressRepository` rewrites only the matching day, never replacing an unreadable file. A failure there is logged and doesn't undo the deleted sessions. Beranda reloads its progress when it appears (`HomeView.task` → `DailyPlanViewModel.load()`), so today's plan picks up the change before it saves again.
- **Shared sessions.** Plan and Balancing sessions hold one exercise, but older routines and finger routines can hold several. Sessions are deleted whole, because summaries are immutable. When any of the day's sessions also practised another exercise, the dialog says those are deleted too.
- **Refresh.** `ReportsViewModel` conforms to a new `PracticeHistoryProviding`. `PracticeDayViewModel` now reads its exercises from it instead of a snapshot, and the panel refreshes it after deleting, so the card leaves the day page and the calendar updates. If any session fails to delete, the panel stays open with an error, after still refreshing so partial deletions show.

Verification: a Debug Simulator build with `IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0` succeeded. Pending, because recording sessions needs the camera: on the baseline iPad, in both languages, delete a plan arm-raise record and a finger record. Then check that the panel closes, the card leaves, the calendar status changes, the session files and `daily-plan-progress.json` entry are gone, Beranda shows the item as not done, Cancel keeps everything, and an emptied day shows its empty message.

## Button click after another owner deactivates (2026-11-23)

The console still showed `AVAudioSession_iOS.mm:978 This method can lead to UI unresponsiveness if called on the main thread` at `GuidanceAudioService.playButtonSound()` (`prepareToPlay()`/`play()`). The click checked a flag on `GuidanceAudioService` that only that service set and cleared. `SessionAudioService.stop()` deactivates the shared session through `AudioInputWorker.stop(deactivate: true)` without clearing it, so after a guided session the flag still read active, the click skipped the awaited activation, and `AVAudioPlayer` activated the session synchronously on the main thread.

- **Shared state.** `NarrationAudioSession.isSessionActive` reads the session's real state under its lock. `playButtonClick()` checks it in place of the removed per-service flag, so the first tap after any owner's deactivation waits for `activate()`.
- **Not changed.** Cue and count players run only inside a finger session after `configure(language:)` awaited activation, and nothing else deactivates during it.

Verification: Debug Simulator build. Pending on the M1 iPad: no main-thread warning when tapping buttons after a guided, finger and Balancing session and at launch, and the click still plays (the first tap after a session slightly later).

## Music and button click off the main thread (2026-11-23)

The console still showed `AVAudioSession_iOS.mm:978 This method can lead to UI unresponsiveness if called on the main thread` at `ExerciseMusicPlayer.refresh()` (`AVAudioPlayer(contentsOf:)`/`prepareToPlay()`) and `GuidanceAudioService.playButtonSound()` (`prepareToPlay()`/`play()`). Checking `NarrationAudioSession.isSessionActive` (*Button click after another owner deactivates*) is not enough: the player still calls into the audio session when it loads, prepares and plays, and a deactivation can land between the check and the call. `refresh()` also runs on every `UserDefaults` change. Cues, counts and narration never warned because `PreparedAudioClip` already runs its player on its own queue.

- **Button click.** `GuidanceAudioService` plays `button-click.wav` through a `PreparedAudioClip` at volume 0.18; the clip rewinds on each play as the old player did.
- **Music.** `ExerciseMusicPlayer` keeps its main-actor state and API. Every `AVAudioPlayer` call (load, prepare, loop, play, pause, rewind, ducking fade) runs on a private `MusicWorker` serial queue, in the order requested. A failed load clears the track on the main actor, so the next refresh retries, as before. The 500 ms startup check calls the worker's play-if-stopped, so a playing track is never restarted.
- **Not changed.** The activation gate before the first click and music's awaited activation stay. `SessionAudioService` and `GuidanceAudioService` use the music player as before.

Verification: Debug Simulator build with `IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0`. Pending on the M1 iPad: no main-thread warning for button taps at launch and after each session type, music start in a finger, guided and Balancing session and the long rest, and a track change in Settings. Music should loop, duck under narration, resume at its position and never start after Stop, and the click should still play.

## Progress calendar and day pages (2026-11-21)

The product owner supplied two mockups for the Progress tab (History feature) and asked for three reusable components: a Progress Header Card, a Monthly Calendar and a History Card. The Progress root now shows only the header card and the calendar under "Riwayat latihan"; the weekly summary, latest-session card, four-week chart and session list were removed.

- **Components** (DesignSystem, values and actions only): `TMProgressBadge` (the yellow badge with the blue ribbon and a numbered white disc, drawn in SwiftUI because no asset exists), `TMProgressHeaderCard` (caption, headline, badge; not interactive), `TMMonthlyCalendar` with `TMCalendarDayStatus` (filled yellow circle = completed, dotted yellow ring = partial, small yellow dot = today, greyed future days that cannot be tapped, legend), and `TMHistoryCard` (exercise name and its numbered badge on a pale blue card; a button only when given an action). New tokens: `badgeRibbon`, `badgeRibbonLight`, `historyCard`.
- **Navigation.** `HistoryRoute.practiceDay(Date)` and `HistoryRouter.showPracticeDay(_:)`. A calendar day opens that day. The header card is information only: a first version opened today's page, but after testing on the iPad the product owner asked for it not to be tappable, so it has no button, press effect, click sound or button trait, and `openToday` was removed. `HistoryBuilder.buildPracticeDay(_:)` builds `TMPracticeDayView` ("Perkembangan latihanmu" with the date beneath, the system back button) from the history the tab already loaded, so opening a day reads no files.
- **What a day shows.** `PracticeHistory` (History/Models) joins finger sessions, guided and Balancing sessions and the plan's saved days. A day is *completed* when every item the plan showed that day reached its goal, *partial* when anything was practised otherwise. The day page lists each practised exercise once, in the order first practised, then plan items done without a saved session; a card with a saved session opens its finger or session report, which keeps the clinician reports reachable now that the list is gone. The header counts practice days (completed or partial) in the current calendar week.
- **Storage gap and fix.** `daily-plan-progress.json` held only the latest day (version 1), so full-day completion was lost each new day; sessions alone cannot tell "all of the plan" because Settings can hide items. Version 2 keeps one entry per day (`completed` and the `planned` item IDs, at most 730 days) and reads a version 1 file as one day judged against `DailyPlan.standard`. `DailyPlanProgress.planned` is recorded when an item completes. History reads it through its own `DailyPlanHistoryRepository`, which Home's `LocalDailyPlanProgressRepository` implements; `AppComponent` shares one repository actor between the plan and the calendar. Days before this change have no plan record, so they show as partial (from their sessions) even if every item was done.
- **Tab bar on child pages.** The product owner asked to hide the Beranda/Progres tab bar on "Perkembangan latihanmu", a child of Progress. A first version put `.toolbar(.hidden, for: .tabBar)` on the pushed page; on device testing the bar showed and hid again during the back transition. Now `TMFingerReportsView` sets it once, outside its `NavigationStack`, from the path (hidden whenever anything is pushed, so a report opened from a day card keeps it hidden too), and the bar returns as the pop lands.
- **Day page spacing.** Its content starts 48 pt below the navigation bar (was 24 pt); the design system has no spacing tokens.
- **Day page title.** After testing on the iPad the product owner asked for the mockup's yellow title and a much smaller date. The title is 26 pt bold in `brand` and the date 16 pt regular in `muted` (it had used `.subheadline`, 22 pt, almost the title's 24 pt). This is an explicit exception to *Foundation color palette*'s "no yellow text" rule: yellow on the light canvas is about 1.8:1, below the 4.5:1 text minimum, so the title is not legible for everyone.
- **No "Hapus riwayat".** A first version kept the delete-all-history action under the calendar; the product owner asked to remove it, so the Progress page matches the mockup. `ReportsViewModel.deleteHistory` and `TaskReportsViewModel.deleteAll` went with it; a single session can still be deleted from its report.
- **History card size.** After testing on the iPad the cards were made larger: 26 pt semibold titles (the day title's size), a 100 pt badge, 24 pt vertical padding, 20 pt corners, and the day page is up to 1180 pt wide (was 840 pt).

Verification: `xcodegen generate`, a Simulator build with `IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0` and a Mac Catalyst build succeeded. Pending on the baseline iPad: the tab bar hiding and returning on the day page (and on Mac Catalyst), the calendar and badge layout in both orientations and in English, Dynamic Type at large sizes (the header moves the badge under the text), VoiceOver labels of days and cards, dark mode, a day completed through the plan showing a filled circle, and the upgrade of an existing version 1 progress file.


## Debug sit-to-stand shortcut (2026-11-23)

It uses the existing `TMDebugActionButton` and its DEBUG marker, and calls the existing `HomeViewModelProtocol.begin(from:)` with `BalanceExercise.sitToStand.rawValue`. The normal plan cards remain sequential. The shortcut is disabled when that exercise is hidden, so the plan's fallback for an unknown item cannot accidentally open a different exercise.

The shortcut opens the ordinary sit-to-stand intro, then uses the existing readiness, calibration, counting, pause, save and continuation behavior. It does not complete earlier exercises, reorder the plan or change a preference. The control and its action are entirely under `#if DEBUG`; Release has no shortcut. No exercise engine, tracking, narration service, router or view-model contract was changed.

## Knees-up sit-to-stand detection and posture cue (2026-11-23)

The product owner requested detection with framing from the knees upward and a clearer indicator, scoped to Berdiri dari Posisi Duduk. The activity already used shoulder, hip and knee only. Its knee confidence floor is now 0.2 while shoulder/hip stay at 0.3; the activity-local smoother accepts 0.2. Current-frame evidence is still mandatory, and missing or weaker knees cannot advance calibration or counting. Global body tracking and other exercise engines are unchanged.

A temporary production-code harness reproduced two failures before the fix: a visible knee at confidence 0.25 was rejected on either side, and a shortened live trunk plus a dropped knee could accept a standing capture without actual hip rise. Hip rise now uses actual displacement from the seated hip, scaled by the captured seated trunk length instead of a changing live trunk. Existing hold durations, stand-and-sit repetition rules and milestone sounds remain.

`SitToStandSnapshot` supplies a typed target posture through the existing Balance snapshot. A reusable value-only `TMPostureCue` shows a sitting/standing icon, Duduk/Berdiri, an up/down arrow and Perlahan; calibration holds replace the arrow with Tahan posisi. It hides during missing joints, camera waiting, pause, countdown and completion. These short visual labels are a scoped exception to the previous no-camera-caption rule; existing spoken instructions and clips remain unchanged.

Verification: the temporary harness passes both sides with shoulder/hip/knee only, knee confidence 0.25, full calibration, two complete repetitions, posture targets and milestone counts; missing knees, confidence 0.19 and the false-standing case do not advance. XcodeGen generation, the Debug Simulator build with `IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0`, localization JSON parsing and `git diff --check` passed. Camera confidence and usability on a physical iPad still require acceptance with a stationary camera, a side view and shoulder, hip and knee visible throughout sitting and standing.


## Torso-only sit-to-stand detection (2026-11-23)

The product owner superseded the knees-up requirement: the intended device distance is around 1 m and the minimum visible area should be the torso. Sit-to-stand now accepts a current-frame shoulder and hip on the same anatomical side at confidence 0.3. The knee is optional at 0.2, never inferred, and only supplies a diagnostic hip angle and an initial seated-pose check when visible. An absent knee publishes no diagnostic angle (the existing zero-to-nil metric mapping); it does not block a hold or repetition. The existing smoother, tracking service, other exercises and posture indicator remain unchanged.

All progression now uses actual hip displacement scaled by the captured seated trunk, so the knee appearing/disappearing cannot switch the progress formula. A standing calibration peak requires hip and shoulder rises of at least 0.25 seated trunk lengths, trunk scale within 0.7–1.35 of the seated reference and absolute lean at most 40 degrees. Standing acceptance in practice also corroborates the torso. Calibration holds remain 3 s; hold tolerance is 12% of trunk length. The patient must return to the seated baseline before practice and each repetition remains a stand plus controlled sit.

This is a torso-travel estimate, not independent confirmation of knee extension or seat contact. It cannot tell whether the initial still torso belongs to an already-standing patient when no knee is visible; the instructed seated baseline and subsequent upward travel are required. A stationary camera must keep shoulder and hip visible throughout. Roughly 1 m is a product setup target, not a depth estimate or validated optical range. Existing bundled narration was preserved; the legacy tracking-loss recordings still mention the knee and need a wording/recording update if those cues are retained in the final UX.

Verification: the temporary production-code harness covers both anatomical sides with and without knees, full held calibration, countdown and two stand/sit repetitions, missing-shoulder rejection, absent knee-angle evidence, and rejection of bending/shoulder-only travel/torso foreshortening as standing calibration. Debug Simulator build succeeded. Physical acceptance at approximately 1 m (sitting and standing in both orientations, cropped knees, framing, subject continuity, narration and real movement precision) remains pending.

## Steadier sit-to-stand at 1 m (2026-11-23)

The product owner asked for smoother sit-to-stand detection that never needs the lower legs, ankles or feet, because the team's ideal patient distance is about 1 m. The activity already read only a same-side shoulder and hip (knee optional, ankle never; *Torso-only sit-to-stand detection*), so the work went into why it still dropped or stalled. Expected framing at 1 m, from the capture setup (1280×720 from the front ultra-wide, roughly 78° vertical, about 1.6 m of height visible, iPad at table height): shoulders and hips are inside the frame seated and standing, the knees seated, ankles at or below the lower edge, and the head of a standing adult often above the top. Shoulder and hip therefore stay the required evidence; the shoulder approaching the top edge when a tall patient stands is the remaining framing risk.

A temporary harness outside the repository replayed synthetic side-view sequences (no ankles or feet, knees absent or at confidence 0.25, landmark jitter, hip confidence dropouts, single detector jumps, skipped inference frames) through the production `BodySubjectSelector`, `MovementLandmarkSmoother`/`OneEuroFilter`, `StableHold` and `SitToStandActivity`. Before the change it found two causes:

- **The selector dropped a standing patient.** Standing raises the torso about a trunk length (≈ 0.23 image heights at 1 m), faster than the slow usual-place anchor follows, so the 1.5 × anchor gate rejected the patient mid-rise and nobody was tracked until they sat back down or the 5 s relock passed: 0 of 5 repetitions in every scenario. In a side view the two shoulders nearly overlap, so jitter also swung the shoulder-width ratio past its range. Now the usual place only arbitrates between several people, and a sole person is followed by frame-to-frame continuity alone; the continuity gate is per 1/30 s frame and widens with skipped frames up to three; shoulder widths within 0.03 image heights (about 5 cm at 1 m) always match. With bystanders in view the previous anchor rule still applies.
- **One missed hip restarted every hold.** A frame without the torso cleared the 3 s hold, hid the circle and flashed "tracking lost", so with 8% one-to-three-frame hip dropouts the seated hold never completed. Now a hold survives up to 0.4 s without torso evidence (`StableHold` with `maximumGap` 0.4) but the interval spanning a missing frame earns nothing (`creditedStep` 0 for that sample; otherwise 0.15 s between fresh samples); the drawn hip stays and no message shows for 0.5 s; longer losses clear the hold and the repetition as before. Before the patient has ever been seen the message shows at once.

Three smaller changes remove sources of false or missed transitions: once the seat is captured, only that side's shoulder and hip are followed (the far hip sits at a different height in a side view, so switching sides read as movement); the seated and standing references are the mean hip over each held pose instead of the single frame that ended the seated hold or the highest frame of the trial, so one confirmed detector jump cannot raise the practice target out of reach; and only a knee at confidence ≥ 0.3 may reject the seat as already standing, while a knee at 0.2 still supplies the diagnostic angle. The smoother, its One Euro tuning and the counting thresholds are unchanged.

The non-narrated posture step now says the feet need not be in view, and the camera's accessibility label no longer promises a shoulder–knee–ankle outline. The recorded `sts.trackingLost`, `sitToStand.tracking` and `session.assistance.sitToStand` still ask for the knee (and "head to knees"); they were left unchanged so text and recordings stay matched, and need a narration retake if the knee should no longer be mentioned.

Verification: after the change the harness counts 5 of 5 repetitions in all 13 counting scenarios (clean; five noisy far-side seeds; three weak-knee seeds; three near-side-only seeds with 8% hip dropouts; 12% dropouts), with no lost-subject frames, and counts 0 for sitting still 30 s, leaning forward without standing and dropping into the seat in 0.25 s. Honest-hold checks: a 0.25 s blackout inside the seated hold delays it past 3.25 s, a 0.3 s blackout inside the standing hold delays it by 0.34 s, a 1 s blackout restarts the hold, and a 2 s blackout in practice shows the tracking-lost message and loses only that repetition. Without a reliable knee the harness also confirms the documented limitation that a still standing torso can be captured as the seat. XcodeGen generation, the Debug Simulator build with `IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0` (BUILD SUCCEEDED), String Catalog JSON parsing and `git diff --check` passed. Pending on the M1 iPad at about 1 m: the actual landmark confidences of shoulder, hip and knee seated and standing in a side view, whether a standing patient's shoulder stays in frame at the team's iPad height, inference rate, counting over full sets, subject continuity with and without a bystander, and Stop/Go and narration during practice.


## Sit-to-stand hold arc in step (2026-11-23)

After testing, the product owner reported that the hip circle's hold arc glitched and did not run in step with the seated and standing holds. A per-frame replay in the temporary harness (sit → held stand → sit → two practice repetitions, with one-frame torso dropouts) showed the cause in `SitToStandActivity`, not the view: the standing calibration hold could start as soon as the trial showed a plausible stand (a hip rise of 0.25 seated trunk lengths with an upright torso), which is still mid-rise. Its first sample then fell behind the rising hip, the hold restarted each time the hip left the 12% tolerance, and the arc flickered between empty and a sliver while the up arrow appeared and disappeared (glitch frames from 5.2 to 5.7 s in the replay, before the stand was reached at 5.6 s). Hold time accrued while rising was also credited to "holding the stand". In practice, the rising half's arc was the raw calibrated rise over 0.75, so sitting jitter drew a wobbling sliver and the arc shrank again after the count's pop.

Changes: the trial records when the hip last rose more than 0.03 seated trunk lengths above its previous peak, and the standing hold starts only 0.3 s after that (a running hold is kept; its tolerance handles drift). Both calibration holds pass `wobbleGrace` 0.25 s to `StableHold`, so one stray sample no longer restarts a hold; stray samples earn nothing. The rising half's arc now runs from the seated threshold (0.2) to the standing threshold (0.75) of calibrated rise, mirroring the lowering half, so it starts empty. The view, `TMTherapyMergingGuide` animation and engine mapping are unchanged. A frame without the torso still freezes the arc without credit (*Steadier sit-to-stand at 1 m*).

Verification: the per-frame replay reports no arc reversals or jumps outside pops and phase changes; the seated arc fills 0 → 1 over 3 s through a missed frame, the standing hold starts at 5.77 s (stand reached at 5.6 s) and pops at 8.77 s, and the countdown arc fills over 3 s through a missed frame. The full regression still counts 5 of 5 in every counting scenario and 0 in the false-count scenarios; honest-hold checks pass (a 0.3 s blackout delays the standing hold by 0.33 s). With 12% hip dropouts a 4 s calibration stand is no longer enough to finish the 3 observed seconds (it previously passed only because rising time was credited); a patient who stays standing until the hold pops calibrates and counts 5 of 5. XcodeGen generation and the Debug Simulator build with `IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0` passed. Pending on the M1 iPad at about 1 m: how the arc looks and feels in both holds and both practice halves with real landmark noise, whether the 0.3 s arrival settle feels responsive, and whether real patients stand long enough for the standing hold when tracking drops frames.

## Debug start any exercise and skip rest (2026-11-23)

The team tests on an iPad and asked for faster debugging: "add more debug features for start with any exercise and skip on every rest". Both tools are Debug-only, wear the existing `.debugOnly()` marker and are compiled out of Release.

**Start any exercise.** Beranda's leading toolbar gains a `TMDebugMenu`, "Mulai latihan mana pun" / "Start any exercise", next to the sit-to-stand shortcut (kept, since the product owner asked for it by name). It lists every shown plan item in plan order, numbered and with a done mark, and calls the existing `HomeViewModelProtocol.begin(from:)`, which a tapped card also uses. The chosen item becomes the plan's current index, so its intro, readiness, session, summary, long rest and the following items run exactly as they do from a card; earlier items are not marked done and nothing is reordered. Hidden exercises are not in `rows` and cannot be picked, so `begin(from:)`'s fallback to the first undone item cannot open a different exercise. The plan editor (`DebugPlanLayout`) and the plan intro were considered and rejected: the editor changes saved preferences, and the intro is only reached once an item is already chosen.

**Skip rest.** `TMDebugSkipRestButton` (DesignSystem) puts "Lewati istirahat" / "Skip rest" at the bottom centre of every rest page, so it never meets the header's "Finish exercise". It shows only while the rest runs (not paused, not waiting for the camera) and each skip is a command on the engine or view model that owns that rest:

- Guided sessions (light and dark layouts, timed breathing and the Indonesian arm-raise script's breaths): `SessionCommand.debugSkipRest`. `SessionEngine.skipRestForDebug()` cancels speech, clears the rest breath and replaces the rest with an elapsed timed rest carrying the same `recalibrateBeforeNext`, then runs `tick()`, which takes the branch an expired rest takes (`advanceAfterRest()`, then the next set, recalibration or the next activity). The scripted `BreathingRest` leaves with the old stage, so none of its later steps announce. It is ignored unless `canProgress`, so it never lifts a pause.
- Balancing: `BalanceSessionViewModel.skipRestForDebug()` cancels the rest task (its sleep throws, so `runRest()` returns without calling `startNextSet()` itself), cancels speech and calls `startNextSet()`, which leads into the next set's countdown.
- Finger and forearm sessions: the breathing rest pages (timed and the scripted opposition breaths) call the session's existing `skipRest()`, the same command the set and "next up" pages already offer during a rest. It sets the rest's remaining time to zero; the next tick drops the scripted breathing and starts the next set's countdown, handing narration off as an elapsed rest does.
- The plan's long rest (`TMDailyPlanNextView`): its stages live in the page, so the skip stops music and speech and jumps the page to `.countdownIntro`, the stage the loop reaches after naming the next exercise, and restarts the transition loop (its `task` id includes a Debug-only skip count, because the loop keeps its start time locally). An unscripted next exercise then shows its ten-second countdown with "Lewati hitung mundur" / "Skip countdown" at the top trailing corner, which cancels speech and calls `launchAfterRest()`, exactly as the countdown's end does; the plan clears its resting flag, so a late loop iteration cannot launch again. A scripted next intro has no countdown; its existing "Start without narration" skips the brief. The long rest between the two sides of a one-side-per-session exercise uses the same page and the same skip.

Rest skipping changes no timing, threshold, saved result or narration in normal use; Release keeps the earlier `task` id (`paused || scenePhase != .active`).

Verification: XcodeGen generation, Debug and Release Simulator builds with `IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0` (both BUILD SUCCEEDED), String Catalog JSON parsing and `git diff --check` passed. The Debug app's `Tuntun.debug.dylib` contains `TMDebugSkipRestButton` and `skipRestForDebug`; no file of the Release app does. Source review traced each skip into the same end-of-rest code an elapsed rest runs. Nothing was run in the Simulator or on the iPad. Pending on the M1 iPad: the start-any-exercise menu opening each plan item and the plan continuing to the following items; Skip rest in a guided body session (timed breathing, a recalibrating next set, the next bilateral side), during the Indonesian arm-raise and opposition breaths, in Balancing and in a finger session; the long-rest skip and the countdown skip, including between the two sides of a one-side-per-session exercise and before a scripted next intro; that no breath line or countdown number plays after a skip; and both languages.

## Practice time for every exercise (2026-11-23)

The user reported that the exercise detail panel's "Waktu bergerak" (time moving) always read 0 for some exercises. The tile sums each set's `SessionActivityResult.practiceSeconds`, but only directional reach (`ReachSnapshot.practiceSeconds`) and timed practice filled it. `SessionEngine.practicedSeconds` returned 0 for movement and fold towel, and `BalanceSessionLog.recordSet` never set it. So arm raise, elbow bend, fold towel and every Balancing exercise saved 0. Average seconds per repetition fell back to that 0 for sessions without every repetition's metrics, so it showed "–" there.

- **Credit rule.** The existing reach rule now applies everywhere: each fresh tracked practice frame credits at most 0.2 s, and a gap over 0.3 s credits nothing. Pauses, camera waits, lost tracking, calibration and countdowns are never credited.
- **Movement** (`MovementSnapshot.practiceSeconds`): credited in `MovementActivity.consume` on scored practice frames once calibrated, not during the countdown or the return before the first one. It resets on the next set.
- **Fold towel** (`TowelSnapshot.practiceSeconds`): credited on measured frames in the practice phase. It resets on the next set and is kept through a restart (recalibrate or adjust target).
- **Engine.** `ActivityPresentation.practiceSeconds` gives each presentation's figure. `SessionEngine.practicedSeconds` reads it, and `AlternatingSidesActivity` sums both sides for movement, towel and reach.
- **Balancing.** `BalanceSessionViewModel.record(_:previous:)` credits counted frames that have an overlay. `recordSet` saves the total through the new `practiceSeconds` parameter of `BalanceSessionLog.recordSet`, then resets it. The pause dialog's `SessionDurationClock` is unchanged, because it also counts rests and waits.
- **Earlier history.** `ExerciseDetailViewModel` uses a set's summed `RepMetrics.duration` when its saved practice time is 0, so sessions saved before this change no longer show 0. Stored files are not migrated.
- The guided session summary now shows elapsed seconds for a movement or towel set with practice time but no repetition, as it already did for reach.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug iOS Simulator build with `IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0` succeeded. Nothing was run in the Simulator or on the iPad. Pending on the M1 iPad: arm raise, elbow bend (one side and both), fold towel and one Balancing exercise, each showing a nonzero time moving close to the active practice time; a pause and a tracking loss mid-set not adding time; a multi-set session crediting each set separately; and a day recorded before the change showing the backfilled figure.


## Tracking status chip (2026-11-23)

At the user's request, the camera preview shows a white chip in its top-left corner while an exercise runs. It reads "Posisi sudah pas" / "In position" with a green dot (`success`) while tracking is good, and "Posisi belum pas" / "Not in position yet" with a red dot (`warning`) while it is not. The wording also changes between the two states, so colour is never the only signal.

- **Component.** `TMTrackingStatusChip` and the `.trackingStatusChip(_:videoBounds:)` modifier (DesignSystem/Components/TherapySessionStatus.swift) take values only. The chip sits 20 pt inside the visible video, so it never lands in a letterbox margin. It stays light in dark mode, takes no taps and crossfades over 0.25 s, with no animation under Reduce Motion.
- **Guided light sessions** (arm raise, elbow bend, directional reach, fold towel). Good means the presentation has no `trackingMessage`, which the activities already debounce: lost after 1 s, found after 0.5 s. The chip shows where the body indicator does: the opening cues, calibration and practice. It is hidden during full-screen stages, pauses and camera waits.
- **Balancing.** `BalanceSnapshot.trackingLost` is set by each engine on its existing missing-body paths. `BalanceSessionViewModel.trackingPositioned` debounces it with the same 1 s / 0.5 s timings. The chip is hidden while preparing, paused, waiting for the camera, resting, counting down, after the last set and on a capture failure.
- **Finger and forearm sessions.** Good means `trackingReady`, which `FingerTrackingRecovery` already settles. The chip is hidden during the countdown, feedback, rest and finished phases, and also while paused, waiting for the camera or showing a recovery error.
- The chip is status, not instruction text, so the rule against instruction text over the camera stands.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug iOS Simulator build with `IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0` succeeded. Nothing was run in the Simulator or on the iPad. Pending on the M1 iPad: in each session type, both languages and both landscape orientations, check that stepping out of frame turns the chip red after about 1 s and back to green on return. Also check that it never covers the bubbles or the finger recovery actions, and that it reads well with large text.


## Set list under the demonstration (2026-11-23)

At the user's request, the live exercise screen (`TMTherapyExerciseLiveLayout`, shared by finger, Balancing and light guided sessions) was reworked:

- **Set list.** The "Repetisi 3 / 8 | Set 1 / 2" counters under the camera were removed (`TMTherapyProgressStat` had no other caller and was deleted). The side column now shows the demonstration, then `TMTherapySetProgress`, then Pause and End. The set list is "Set N" over one circle per repetition for every set, top-aligned. Each row draws exactly its goal's circles, spread with space between them at the size twelve would need. Done repetitions are green with a check and the rest grey. Earlier sets are drawn full, since a set advances only on its goal. It stays hidden, with its space kept, during calibration.
- **Text removed.** The live screen no longer shows the "Ikuti gerakan ini" / step title above the demonstration, or the "Latihan N" eyebrow over the exercise title. Intro, summary and rest pages keep "Latihan N". A new demonstration step still highlights the panel and is announced to VoiceOver.
- **Camera fills.** The camera takes the whole left column. Both previews use `.resizeAspectFill`, and the overlay mappings switched together from fit to fill scale: `CameraDisplayGeometry` (bubbles, guide arrows, fold towel), `fixedBubbleRadius` (acceptance stays the drawn bubble) and `TMFingerLandmarkOverlay`. The image's cropped sides are tracked but not shown.
- **Settings ranges.** Settings' sets are 0–3 and repetitions 0–12 (`DailyPlanOverrides.setsRange`, `repetitionsRange`). A 0 hides the exercise from Beranda and the day's count (`DailyPlanOverrides.hidesExercise`), and Settings marks it Hidden while its controls stay open, so raising it to 1 shows it again. While a plan runs, the visible items update after `end()`. Every plan session clamps its counts to 1–3 and 1–12 (`playableSets`, `playableRepetitions`), including a program's own values and the arm-raise script. Stored routine and history validation (1–5, 1–20) is unchanged, so saved files stay readable.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug iOS Simulator build with `IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0` succeeded. Nothing was run in the Simulator or on the iPad. Pending on the M1 iPad:
- a three-set session in a guided exercise, a Balancing exercise and a finger exercise, with circles filling per repetition and the next set starting its row;
- Pause and End reachable on the smallest iPad and with large text;
- bubbles, arrows, fingertip circles and the fold-towel zones still on the joints with the filled preview, in both orientations;
- reach left/right, cross midline and bilateral arm raise targets staying on screen despite the crop;
- Settings 0 hiding and restoring an exercise.


## One history card per session (2026-11-23)

At the user's request, an exercise practised more than once on a day now shows once per session in the day's history list. Before this, the list showed one card per exercise. Every run was already saved as its own session (a fresh UUID each), but `PracticeHistory` kept only the first session per exercise and the detail panel joined them all. This supersedes "one `TMHistoryCard` per practised exercise" and the whole-day scope of the panel and its delete (logs: *Progress calendar and day pages*, *Exercise detail panel*, *Delete an exercise's day record*).

- **List.** `PracticedExercise` names its exercise as `exerciseID`, and its `id` is the exercise and session together. `PracticeHistory.day` dedupes on that pair, so one session's several sets of an exercise stay one card. A plan item completed without a saved session keeps a single card.
- **Card.** Cards stay title and number only. A start time under the title was tried and dropped at the user's request, so `TMHistoryCard` is unchanged.
- **Panel.** `ExerciseProgress` takes an optional `focus` report. With one, the day's figures, key repetitions and trend point are that session's, and the baselines and trend read every session before it, that day's earlier runs included. "Sesi sebelumnya" on a second run therefore compares it with the first. A plan-only card keeps the whole-day view.
- **Delete.** "Hapus catatan latihan ini" deletes the shown session only. The plan's done mark is removed only when no other session of that exercise is left on the day.

Verification: XcodeGen and a Debug iOS Simulator build with `IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0` succeeded. Nothing was run in the Simulator or on the iPad. Pending: practise arm raise and a finger exercise twice each on one day, then check four numbered cards, each panel showing its own run, the previous-session comparison, and deleting one run keeping the other card and the done mark.

`main` was merged into `feat/adjustment`. Both branches had rewritten the exercise detail panel. Each side's SYSTEM_DESIGN.md text covered only its own changes, so §8 now describes both. This supersedes both one-sided descriptions.

- **Kept from `feat/adjustment`** (*Exercise detail demonstration and forgiving consistency*): the looping demonstration in place of the two poses, only the hardest key repetition shown, and Kestabilan at 10/15.
- **Kept from `main`** (*One history card per session*, *Exercise detail insights at full width*, *Neutral history card and calendar surfaces*): one card and panel per session, deleting only the shown session, insights at full width under the demonstration and headline, and the Progress surfaces.
- **Code.** In the narrow layout of `TMExerciseProgressSection`, the demonstration (at most 340 points wide) now sits above `change`. The insights card follows at full width, because `main` removed `summary`.

Verification: a Debug Simulator build after the merge. Pending on the baseline iPad: the panel checks listed under both source entries.

## Freeze at set end and rest end (2026-11-23)

The user reported that the screen still froze for a moment when an exercise or a rest finished, with or without the Debug Skip rest. The fix in *Faster exit after End* (commit b5a61912) is still in place, but it only traced Pause → End. A source trace of the automatic set end, rest start and end, summary and long rest found the following main-thread blocking work. None of it was measured, so this list is the source analysis, not profiler output.

- **Audio session lock.** `NarrationAudioSession` held its one lock across `setCategory`, `setActive(true)`, `setActive(false)` and the speaker override. The background deactivation from *Faster exit after End* therefore still blocked the main thread, which takes the same lock for `isSessionActive` (every button tap), `activationToken` and `activate()` (long rest, music). The lock now guards only `activationRequests` and `isActive`. A separate `hardwareLock`, taken only on `sessionQueue` and the input worker's queue, orders the blocking calls. Deactivation compares its token under that lock, so an activation counted in between waits, finds the session inactive and reactivates it. `handleRouteChange()` now runs on `sessionQueue`, and the route-change configuration log reads the session off the main thread.
- **Camera preview.** `TMBodyCameraPreview.updateUIView` requested a layout on every SwiftUI update, and the guided screen updates on every 50 ms tick and every frame. Each layout touched the preview connection, which waits while the capture session stops (summary) or reconfigures (fold towel's lens switch). It now relays out only when mirroring or the video mask changes; size, window and `isPreviewing` changes still request their own. Mirroring is set only when it differs.
- **Finger listening engine.** `GuidanceAudioService.disableVoice()` stopped its `AVAudioEngine` and removed the tap on the main thread at a finger session's natural finish. The service now uses `AudioInputWorker`, as the guided and Balancing sessions do. The engine starts once in `enableVoice()`, and a restarted recognition only replaces the request its tap feeds.
- **Finger cues.** `playCue` and `playNumber` built a new `AVAudioPlayer` on the main thread for every hold pop, set end, countdown number and hold second. They now play cached `PreparedAudioClip`s, which load and play off the main thread; `configure(language:)` preloads the effects and the first ten counts and countdowns.
- **Rest shaders.** SwiftUI compiles a Metal shader the first time it is drawn, which stalled the first rest of each launch. `TMShaderWarmUp` (DesignSystem) draws the breathing circle and long-rest waves at 2 pt and 1% opacity beneath `TMLoadingOverlay`'s backdrop, so they compile while a session prepares. `Shader.compile(as:)` needs iOS 18 and is not used.
- **Smaller costs.**
  - The DEBUG `Thread.callStackSymbols` in `stopListening()` (every summary) is removed.
  - Both `cancelSpeech()` methods call `stopSpeaking(at: .immediate)` only while the synthesizer is speaking. Every countdown number cancels first.
  - `NarrationVoice.best(for:)` caches its choice per language, because `speechVoices()` is slow.
  - `SessionAudioService.prepare()` returns early when its clips are already loaded for the language, so the long rest no longer reloads about 35 players.
  - `ExerciseMusicPlayer.stop()` pauses and rewinds instead of releasing the player, so a session's end and the long rest neither tear it down nor reload it on the main thread.
- **Not done.**
  - The loading overlay was not added to the automatic finishes. Their teardown no longer blocks the main thread, and the overlay would flash over a summary that is already shown.
  - `SessionSnapshot` is not made Equatable to skip redundant writes; whether to do so depends on device profiling.

Verification: XcodeGen, then Debug and Release Simulator builds with IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0 (both BUILD SUCCEEDED). Nothing was run in the Simulator or on the iPad. Pending on the M1 iPad, measured with Instruments Hangs and Time Profiler:
- arm raise with two sets: set end, rest, Skip rest and a timed rest, summary, long rest;
- the same path in a Balancing exercise;
- a finger exercise: set end, rest, natural finish;
- fold towel starting and ending;
- the first rest's breathing circle appearing without a hitch;
- narration not muted in the next session;
- Stop/Go after a rest, and in a finger session after a recognition restart;
- button clicks after a session;
- finger cues, countdowns and hold seconds audible at their usual levels;
- music restarting from the start of the track after a session;
- preview mirroring in both orientations;
- no `AVAudioSession … main thread` console warning.


## Partial daily-plan history on Beranda (2026-11-23)

The product owner clarified that a yellow dashed marker means an exercise is currently actionable, while a yellow checklist means its required repetitions and completion goal were actually reached. Beranda now preserves that distinction across the day boundary.

- `HomeDayState.partial` represents a past day with at least one, but not every, planned exercise ID in `DailyPlanProgress.completed`. No completion is `missed`; every planned ID completed is `completed`.
- On today's plan, the first unfinished exercise remains dashed and actionable. Leaving its session before the goal calls `finish(completed: false)`, so its ID is not inserted and the same item stays next.
- On a past partial day, completed item IDs retain their yellow checklist. Unfinished items are grey and disabled because the past day cannot be resumed; a dashed marker is never used for a disabled item.
- The partial subtitle is "Ada beberapa latihan yang terlewatkan, coba lagi di sesi berikutnya!" in Indonesian and "Some exercises were missed. Try again in the next session!" in English. Today's current state continues to show the completed-item count.

## Tracking chip live through narration (2026-11-23)

The product owner noted that the tracking-status chip depends on vision tracking, so tracking must stay on while narration plays, in every exercise, and asked for the chip to look slightly muted while repetitions are not being counted. They chose to keep it at full strength whenever movement is acted on (calibration holds and practice) and mute it only while movement is ignored.

- **Cause.** The chip read tracking state that only scored frames refreshed. While a cue held evidence back, `MovementActivity` and `DirectionalReachActivity` moved their guide on previews but left `trackingMessage` frozen, `FoldTowelActivity` ignored previews, and Balancing skipped sit-to-stand frames entirely, so `trackingLost` went stale.
- **Live status.** Movement and reach previews now run the same 1 s loss / 0.5 s recovery debounce. Movement remembers its last preview with the limb found, so the first tick after a cue does not date a loss from the last scored frame. Fold towel follows the exercised hand on previews with its 0.1 s bridge, without touching grip, cursor or fold sequence. `BalanceMovementEngine` gains `preview(_:)`: the arm engines keep consuming unscored frames, and sit to stand sets `trackingLost` from shoulder-and-hip evidence on either side (`SitToStandActivity.hasTorsoEvidence`), never suspending its activity, which would restart its holds and countdown. Finger sessions already tracked every sample.
- **Muted chip.** `TMTrackingStatusChip(isMuted:)` / `.trackingStatusChip(_:muted:videoBounds:)` fades to 0.55 opacity with the same colours and wording. The guided session mutes it in the instruction, demonstration and preparation stages and while its presentation counts down. Balancing mutes it while `advanceOpeningIfReady` holds practice back. Finger sessions mute it during the calibration intro and whenever `FingerActivity.actsOnMovement` is false (a stage waiting for its narration, except the range sweep, which measures during its instruction, and the demonstration). Full-screen countdowns still hide it.

Verification: `xcodegen generate` and an iOS Simulator build with `IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0` succeeded. Nothing was run in the Simulator or on the iPad. Pending on the baseline iPad: in arm raise, directional reach, fold towel, sit to stand, a Balancing arm exercise and a finger exercise, step out of view and back during the opening narration and see the muted chip change within about 1 s and 0.5 s; confirm full strength once calibration starts; confirm no "Posisi belum pas" flash when a cue ends while in position; confirm frames during narration still credit nothing.

## Smooth thumb-opposition circles (2026-11-23)

In Sentuh jempol ke jari (thumb opposition), the thumb and target circles moved less smoothly than the other exercises' indicators. The difference was on screen. `TMTherapyMergingGuide`'s `glide` worked only for a single circle (*Whole-hand circle follows the grip*), so the two-circle opposition guide animated only `merge`. Its centres jumped once per 30 Hz camera frame, and its diameter changed in 4 pt steps.
- **Design system.** `glide` now also applies to the two-circle form. A private animatable layer interpolates both centres and both diameters outside the merging layer, so callers without `glide` (fold towel, body bubbles) are unchanged. Reduce Motion still places them at once.
- **Overlay.** `TMFingerLandmarkOverlay` passes the whole-hand circle's 0.15 s spring without bounce (`circleGlide`) to the opposition guide, and sizes the pair without the 4 pt rounding. The separate fingertip bubbles keep their stepped sizes. The merge thresholds, `merge` spring and scoring are unchanged; this is presentation only.

## Fold towel inside the filled preview (2026-11-23)

After the camera previews switched to aspect-fill (*Set list under the demonstration*), the fold towel's grab and goal zones (x 0.2 / 0.8, y 0.2 / 0.8 of the image) and its 0.6-wide towel fell partly into the image's cropped sides, so the indicator was cut off. The demonstration, which reuses `TMTowelWorkspaceView`, was cropped the same way.

- **Workspace is the visible preview.** `CameraDisplayGeometry.visibleRect` gives the part of the image the fill preview shows, in normalized image coordinates. `FoldTowelActivity` maps the filtered palm cursor into it (`workspacePoint`), so the sequence, the snapshot's hand position and acceptance all use workspace coordinates whose 0…1 spans exactly the visible view. `HandCursorFilter` still runs in image coordinates, keeping its tuning. Without a viewport yet the workspace is the whole image; a viewport change already suspends the sequence.
- **Drawing.** `TMTowelWorkspaceView` maps workspace points straight onto its own frame (`TowelBoard.screen`) and sizes the towel and zones from the frame, not the overflowing image rect. The demonstration now fills its panel.
- **Tradeoff.** The fixed path keeps its fractions of the visible view, so on a cropped column the physical reach is shorter in proportion to the crop (a 16:9 image in a column about 1.2 wide: the 0.6 reach is about 0.4 of the image width). Keeping the whole path on screen requires it.

Verification: `xcodegen generate` and a Debug iOS Simulator build with `IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0` succeeded. Nothing was run in the Simulator or on the iPad. Pending on the M1 iPad: in both orientations and on both sides, the towel, chevrons and both zones fully visible for horizontal and vertical folds; the hand circle staying on the palm and joining the zone where it is drawn; four folds completing; the demonstration uncropped.

The Debug-only Beranda menu can preview `.missed`, `.current`, `.partial` and `.completed` without waiting for another calendar day.
